# Validation of the 0.28.0 port (PR #43 head 06b3185 + one build repair), 2026-09-03

Image built from this branch (`v0.28-validation`), RTX 4090 under WSL2 (24 GB, `GPU_UTIL=0.93`), the launchers as
shipped on this branch, `PREFIX_CACHE=1`, compile cache disabled on every boot. A second machine (native RTX 3090,
Ubuntu) runs the same cells; its rows are cited by commit when they land. Everything here is one boot per cell.

## Boot matrix, with the maintainer's exact-output probe

The probe: greedy `chat/completions`, thinking off, "count from 1 to 30 as a comma-separated list", PASS when the
reply is exactly the sequence. `fast` (bf16) runs at `MAX_LEN=57344` here: at the launcher's 65,536 default the
KV need (4.76 GiB) exceeds this WSL2 card's budget at 0.93 (4.71 GiB); the native 3090 boots the default.

| cell | launcher | boot | pool (tokens) | probe | warnings of note |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| bf16 + MTP | start_qwen.sh fast mtp | 186 s | 63,799 | PASS | padding layers 6.25% |
| bf16 + DFlash2 | start_qwen.sh fast dflash2 | 196 s | 67,048 | PASS | padding layers up to 60% |
| bf16, no spec | start_qwen.sh fast off | 166 s | 98,249 | PASS | |
| int8 + MTP | start_qwen.sh long mtp | 186 s | 159,183 | PASS | FULL_AND_PIECEWISE not supported with spec-decode for this backend; padding 6.25% |
| int8 + DFlash2 | start_qwen.sh long dflash2 | 197 s | 136,429 | PASS | padding up to 60% |
| int8, no spec | start_qwen.sh long off | 165 s | 186,340 | PASS | |
| KVarN + MTP | start_qwen.sh huge mtp | 258 s | 212,389 | PASS | padding 6.25% |
| KVarN + DFlash2 | start_qwen.sh huge dflash2 | 267 s | 298,244 | PASS (short probe only; see the ladder) | fine-grained prefix hits disabled (sliding-window manager); padding up to 60% |
| KVarN, no spec | start_qwen.sh huge off | 236 s | 363,461 | PASS | |
| int4 + DFlash2 | alternative.sh dflash2 | 197 s | 258,836 | PASS | fine-grained prefix hits disabled; padding up to 60% |
| int4, no spec | alternative.sh off | 175 s | 387,742 | PASS | |

Reading, so far: every cell boots, including the KVarN cells the reshape crash used to kill; the count probe passes
everywhere, which is expected to be too easy for the KVarN + DFlash2 cell (the maintainer saw degradation at depth
7 on a longer answer and token soup at depth 15; the ladder below uses four longer probes). Two warnings are new
against 0.27.1 and need the capture-mode story re-proven rather than assumed: the int8 + MTP cell (and only that cell of the eleven) logs that full CUDA
graphs are unsupported with speculative decoding for its attention backend, and the DFlash2 cells report up to 60%
KV padding waste against 6.25% under MTP. For comparison, 0.27.1 current main with the same int4 settings pools
252,573 tokens at `MAX_LEN=244224` on this card; the 0.28 port with the connector attached pooled 256,892.

## Flightbench tier table on the port (in progress)

Same benches and settings as the 0.27.1 table on the flightbench results page (judgment v4, eight decisions; mission
v6.1, eighteen; thinking off at a 900-token reply cap, on at 6,000; two mission runs per cell). 0.27.1 rows in
parentheses where the same cell exists there.

| tier | spec | thinking | judgment (8) | mission (18), per run | mission wall |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| bf16 | DFlash2 | off | 6/8 (8/8) | 15/18, 15/18 (16, 16) | 70 s (71 s) |
| bf16 | DFlash2 | on | 8/8 (6/8) | 16/18 (15, 15) | 66 s (170 s) |
| int8 | DFlash2 | off | 6/8 (6/8) | 15/18, 15/18 (15, 15) | 156 s (80 s) |
| int8 | DFlash2 | on | 6/8 (6/8) | 13/18, 15/18 (17, 17) | 107 s, 84 s (214 s) |
| KVarN | MTP | off | 6/8 | 15/18, 15/18 | 50 s, 52 s |
| KVarN | MTP | on | 6/8 | 18/18, 16/18 | 186 s, 99 s |
| KVarN | DFlash2 | off | 6/8 (8/8) | **8/18, 9/18** (16, 18) | **725 s, 748 s** (430 to 450 s) |
| KVarN | DFlash2 | on | 6/8 (8/8) | **9/18** (16, 14) | **1,335 s** (968 to 1,028 s) |
| int4 | DFlash2 | off | 6/8 (8/8 3D off; 6/8 whale) | 16/18, 16/18 (14, 14 3D off) | 187 s (73 s) |
| int4 | DFlash2 | on | 6/8 (6/8 3D off) | 18/18, 18/18 (18, 18 3D off) | 331 s (535 s) |

Two behaviours in these rows are the version's, not a tier's:

- **The mission's tool loop runs to its per-turn cap far more often.** With thinking off, the bf16 run on
  0.27.1 made 77 tool calls over the twelve turns and every turn ended with a reply; on the port the same
  cell made 144 calls with six turns ending at the 16-call cap and an empty reply (int8: 172 calls, nine
  capped turns). On the capped turns the model polls the same two tools repeatedly (`telemetry sps` and
  `request_evaluation sps`, seven times each on turn two) instead of answering the turn's question, which
  0.27.1 answered directly with no tool call. Scores survive because the scorer credits decisions made on
  later turns; the wall time on int8 doubles. Diagnostic cells are queued (the same cell with prefix caching
  off, with speculation off, with MTP, and int4 with prefix caching off) to find which layer of the port
  changes this. The tool-call parser is the same on both versions (`qwen3_coder`).
- **Judgment misses D6/D7 (arm, then get Houston's go before the final log) on six of eight rows** (int4 with
  thinking off misses D3/D4 instead, as it did on 0.27.1). On 0.27.1 the D6/D7 pair is missed on some rows and
  not others; on the port the model does not arm at all on those rows. Same family as the point above: the model's behaviour in the tool loop, not the decision logic.
- Thinking on: the port's reasoning traces are shorter per turn (288 to 6,285 characters on bf16 against
  1,458 to 14,190 on 0.27.1), so walls drop with thinking on; the int8 thinking-on pair does not reproduce
  itself (13 then 15), the first non-reproducing pair on this port.

### The collapse, reproduced in a fresh container: KVarN + DFlash2 on the port

The KVarN + DFlash2 cell (start_qwen.sh `CTX=huge SPEC=dflash2`, depth 7, prefix caching on, no connector)
fails the mission the way the int4 production server failed it on 0.27.1 with the offload connector: turn one
is fine (13 tool calls, GO for TLI), then from turn two every reply is `duct Register Register Register...`
to the 900-token cap (`finish_reason=length`, repeat ratio 1.00), and the last turn is symbol soup; 8/18 in
725 s. The string is character-identical to yesterday's collapse. Its judgment run took 97 s where every other
tier's took 12 to 26 s. Same image with MTP instead of DFlash2: clean (15/18 twice; 18/18 with thinking on).
0.27.1 on the same cell yesterday: 16/18 and 18/18. So on the port the collapse is reachable with no connector
at all, on KVarN with DFlash2, from the second turn over a shared prefix. Single-shot probes do not see it
(the boot matrix's count probe passes on this cell; a second machine's four-prompt probes pass on three of
four and flip the structure of a short JSON answer at depth 7). This is the maintainer's "wrong output"
cell, with the failure shape named: a Mamba-state collapse on the second turn, not a token swap. The
localisation ladder now runs the mission on this cell with prefix caching off, the fused verify off, lookup
drafting off, the adaptive length off, at depth 15, with speculation off, a repeat, and the same cell on the
0.27.1 image as the control.

### Two machines on the KVarN cell

Same mission, same launcher and settings, only the drafter changed. The second machine is a native RTX 3090
under Ubuntu (a venv install of the same branch, no container).

| machine | KVarN + MTP | KVarN + DFlash2 | shape of the DFlash2 failure |
|---|---|---|---|
| RTX 4090, WSL2, container | 15/18, 15/18 (50 s) | 8/18, 9/18 (725 s, 748 s) | "duct Register" repeated to the cap from turn two |
| RTX 3090, native, venv | 15/18 (77 s) | 3/18 (203 s) | the same string, ten of twelve replies, from the first reply |

One symptom on both machines (an earlier reading of the second machine's row as "coherent but failing" came
from scanning the bench's console output, which carries scores and tool calls but no reply text; the trace
file has the collapse string throughout, and that reading is withdrawn). The MTP rows are identical across
the machines, so the benches, launchers and pools are equivalent and the DFlash2 gap is the port's. The
score difference (3 against 8 or 9) is how far the scorer gets before the replies stop being usable. On the
second machine the block-aligned-hits marker was present on all seven of its DFlash2 cells and absent on all
six MTP or no-speculation cells, and the depth-7 JSON divergence on a short prompt reproduced byte-identically
across runs, which is what a deterministic aligned-down continuation would do.

Diagnostic hunks so far (both on this branch under `patches/`, unapplied drafts): suppressing KVarN's
pre-forward flush and reclaim on a wide cached continuation starves the staging pool on the 4090 (engine
dead, "KVarN pool exhausted (32 slots)", eighteen requests in); the flush-only variant applies cleanly on
both machines, does not heal the collapse on the 3090 (3/18 to 3/18, the string still present), and on the 4090 stalls
the engine: 32 requests in, one request running at zero tokens per second indefinitely, no fatal; on the
3090 the same variant completes the whole mission with the score unchanged (3/18, 204 s against 203 s).
So the flush is what frees staging slots on the machine where it stalls, the lifecycle timing is
environment-sensitive in the same way the collapse symptom is, and the fix has to handle the aligned-down
continuation correctly rather than skip a step.

## The connector arm per tier

The 0.27.1 engine-death recipe: the offloading connector, the pool pinned near the working set, and a side-load
of long prompts so the conversation's blocks leave the GPU between turns and come back from CPU. On 0.27.1 every
tier died in the scheduler within minutes; on the port:

| tier | pool | reload batches during the run | engine | mission |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| int4 + DFlash2 | 36,739 | 73 | alive, no assert | 12/18, every turn coherent (judgment misses, not repetition) |
| int8 + DFlash2 | 31,200 | 40 | alive, no assert | 15/18 |
| bf16 + DFlash2 | 20,264 | 21 | alive, no assert | 15/18 (its no-connector score) |
| KVarN + MTP | 57,079 | 14 | alive, no assert | **2/18**: coherent text, the model insists the tracking tool is not available to it (15/18 without the connector) |

Reading: the 0.27.1 engine death does not occur on the port on any tier, and int4, int8 and bf16 keep their
no-connector scores under 21 to 73 reload batches. KVarN does not: with MTP, fourteen reloads are enough for
the model to lose the tool definitions that live in the system prompt's blocks, a different shape from the
repetition collapse: coherent text with lost knowledge rather than a repeated token. Put beside the KVarN + DFlash2
collapse here, both KVarN failures on the port are blocks re-entering KVarN's staging pool from outside the
normal write path (a reload from CPU, or an aligned-down re-prefill over a shared prefix), and the other tiers,
which write through stock slot mappings, do not have the failure. For the offload goal this means: on the
port the CPU offload connector is usable with int4, int8 and bf16, and not with KVarN until KVarN's residency
lifecycle handles re-entering blocks.

### The instrument speaks: what turn two actually recomputes

The third diagnostic routed the wide continuation through KVarN's slow attention path (fp32 SDPA over gathered
history) with the lifecycle untouched. Not healed: 7/18, the same string from turn two, ten of twelve replies
cut at the cap. The attention consumer is exonerated. But the debug gate that rode with it fired 246 times,
and the first prefix-hit request of turn two reads, on this machine:

```
seq_len=1553 query_len=657 committed=896
blocks k=7..12 = physical 840..845, every one slot=None flush=False
```

The conversation is 1,553 tokens and the previous turn ended near 1,540, but the hit is honoured only to 896,
a scheduler-block boundary (neither the 128-token KVarN block nor the true hit). The 657-token remainder is
recomputed as one continuation, and every block it writes into is one KVarN has already flushed to permanent
int4 and released from its fp16 pool (`slot None`). On later turns the pattern repeats: committed stays at 896
or 1,344 while the sequence grows. The int4 tier writes the same rows through stock slot mappings and simply
overwrites them; KVarN has no staging slot for a block it considers finished, and where those rows land is
the poison site. Two threads follow from this: what KVarN does with a write into a slotless block (the fix),
and why the hit stops at 896 on the port when 0.27.1 hit at the true boundary (the cause). The int4 tier's
tool-loop capping is the same continuation seen without corruption: with prefix caching off, int4 answers
turn two directly and caps one turn instead of six; bf16 with MTP caps nine turns, so the capping does not need
DFlash2's drafter group, only the port's prefix-cache resume.

### Two fixes, not one (after round four of the code review)

> **Correction (08:45Z, see "The continuation reading falls" below):** the re-staging half is not the collapse's cause. The same cell with prefix caching off, where no continuation exists, collapses identically. The hit-precision regression stands as a port defect; it is no longer the explanation of the KVarN + DFlash2 collapse.

The debug tuples read as pre-allocation state: KVarN's build re-stages every block the continuation touches
into a fresh fp16 slot and the scatter writes there, so rows are not dropped. The candidate poison site moves
to the block the recompute only partly covers (the last one, rows 1,536 to 1,553 of 128), flushed later as a
whole tile with the reused slot's previous contents in its unwritten rows. The fourth diagnostic clears a
newly assigned slot on allocation and pairs a post-allocation line with the pre-allocation one; its two
cells (alone, and stacked on the slow-path routing) are queued on both machines.

Separately, and confirmed from both trees: the port resumes turn two at 896 because 896 is the scheduler
block (the least common multiple of KVarN's 128-token block and the 448-token Mamba page; the promoted
sliding-window drafter group is excluded from that multiple), and 0.28's coordinator falls back to that
alignment when a manager cannot take a fine-grained hit. The 0.27.1 fork resumed the same configuration at
1,536 through its own sliding-window handling in the runner patch, which the regenerated 0.28 runner patch
does not carry. So there are two fixes: KVarN's re-staging must be correct under any wide continuation (the
correctness half, KVarN only), and the port must restore hit precision for the promoted group (the
regression half, every tier: this is what makes the port's turn two cap the tool loop on bf16, int8 and int4
and re-prefill hundreds of tokens per turn).

## The branch moved while this ran (08:30Z)

Overnight the fork's main merged #67 (the int4 3D scratch allocated inside the memory budget) and the author
rebased #43 onto it with four new commits (a DFlash2 accepted-token accounting change in the lookup and chain
patches, a KVarN "long verify split", and two patch-formatting commits); the maintainer independently
confirmed the earlier build note on 06b3185 and named his blocker as `CTX=huge` + DFlash2 reproducing 0 of
800 tokens verbatim at both verify widths against 800 of 800 on `CTX=fast`. The rebased head 46bad9d does not
build as committed either: the chain patch is malformed at line 350, and with the headers recounted the lookup
patch fails at hunk 15 (speculator.py:414, the hand-edited accounting hunk). Everything above this line was
measured on 06b3185 plus the two repairs; the new head's own rows wait on a patch that applies. The
diagnostic ladder adds two rows since the tables above: with lookup drafting off the KVarN cell still
collapses (8/18, the same string), and bf16 with speculation off and prefix caching on still caps nine of
twelve turns, so the tool-loop regression needs no drafter at all and belongs to the port's prefix caching on
this hybrid.

## The continuation reading falls: prefix caching off still collapses (08:45Z)

Two ladder cells landed that the reading above cannot survive, both KVarN + DFlash2 k=7 on the port, same mission, same box:

| cell | score | turn-two reply | replies cut at the cap |
|---|---|---|---|
| `PREFIX_CACHE=0` (every turn a fresh prefill; no continuation exists) | 7/18 | "duct Register Register ..." | 11 of 12 |
| `VLLM_DFLASH2_LOOKUP_ADAPTIVE=0` | 7/18 | "Flight, I can't uplink that PAD ..." | 10 of 12 |

With prefix caching off there is no aligned-down hit, no re-staging over flushed blocks, nothing for KVarN's continuation path to do, and the collapse is unchanged. So the aligned-down continuation and the re-staging are real (the instrument's tuples stand) but they are not what breaks the text. The round-four patches (clear reused slots; slow path) are demoted to the port's hit-precision regression, which still costs every tier its fine-grained hits.

What does fit: the failure needs a KVarN verify step at long context. Turn one (a 2,670-token prompt) survives; turn two (2,917 tokens, over the tool results) does not; the maintainer's single-request verbatim instrument fails at `CTX=huge` + DFlash2 with no conversation at all. The author's rebase carries a kernel change aimed at exactly that regime. The kernel installed in the validated image already warns, in `kvarn_verify_attention`, that the one-stage `VQ_INDIRECT` kernel "can return finite but incorrect logits" at long context on sm86, and forces the split-K path whenever `KVARN_SPLIT_K=1`; its automatic split still required the verify batch to fit the SM count. The rebased commit 910bbf2 ("long verify split") drops that last condition, so every verify batch at 16 or more context blocks (2,048 tokens) takes the two-stage path. Against the installed file the whole change is one line:

```diff
-    auto_split = (_sw <= 0) and (max_ctx_blocks >= 16) and (NQ * Hk <= sm_count)
+    auto_split = (_sw <= 0) and (max_ctx_blocks >= 16)
```

Wider verify batches (the fork's multi-chain lookup on repetitive turns; concurrent sequences) exceed the SM count and were taking the one-stage kernel exactly when the context passed 2,048 tokens. That is consistent with every row above: short single-shot probes pass, the first long turn mostly passes, the second collapses, both boxes, prefix caching or not.

Under test now, behind the ladder: the mission with that one line applied to the validated image (twice), and the maintainer's own `bench/residue_sweep.py` over all 128 residues, unpatched and then patched, so the fix has a negative control, a positive control, and the raw captures before it is claimed. The other seat is running the same line natively. Nothing about this is posted.

### The 0.27.1 control and two more knobs (08:40Z): a port regression, and not the verify kernel

| cell | image | score | tool calls | replies capped / cut | turn-two reply |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| KVarN + DFlash2 k=7, the 0.27.1 fork | dfee877 | **18/18** | 50 | 0 / 0 | "No. That PAD is void. The pre-launch PAD was computed befor..." |
| same, the port, `KVARN_FUSED_VERIFY=0` | pr43-0.28 | 9/18 | 28 | 0 / 10 | "duct Register Register ..." |
| same, the port, `DFLASH_TOKENS=15` | pr43-0.28 | (running) | | | |

The 0.27.1 fork passes the identical cell on the identical box with nothing capped and nothing cut, so both the collapse and the tool-loop capping are regressions of the port. Fused verify off makes KVarN's builder stop classifying verify steps as decodes; they take the prefill path and `kvarn_verify_attention` never runs, and the text collapses anyway. Sol's round five adds the arithmetic: on this deployment the verify batch is eight tokens over four KV heads, thirty-two programs, below the SM count of either card, so the installed kernel already takes the split path and the rebased head's split-K change alters nothing for a single sequence. The one-line change above stays in the queue only as a formality.

Then the code itself: the installed `kvarn_attn.py` is byte-identical on the two images, and the installed decode kernel differs only by a comment and by what `KVARN_SPLIT_K=0` means. KVarN's own code did not regress. What regressed is what the runner feeds it. The 0.27.1 fork's `kvarn/kvarn-v2-runner.patch` is 361 lines and lists seven allocator and geometry fixes; the port's regenerated `kvarn-v2-runner-0.28.0.patch` is 224 lines with smaller hunks in the DFlash2 speculator, the model runner and the Mamba hybrid state. The accounting change in the rebased head (the emitted-token count) feeds only the adaptive verify-length path, and the adaptive-off cell collapsed too, so it is not the cause alone. Round six of the code review is a function-by-function diff of the two runner patches; the queue here becomes a draft-width dose-response (k=1, 3, 7, 15), the shared-dequant verify kernel, and the maintainer's residue sweep. The other seat is bisecting the patch set natively.

## The fix: one hunk the regenerated runner patch dropped (09:10Z)

The other seat compared the two KVarN runner patches hunk by hunk instead of guessing a mechanism. Eight files, same on both, except one: the 0.27.1 patch touches `model_executor/layers/attention/attention.py` twice and the regenerated 0.28.0 patch once. The missing hunk is the one that, for KVarN cache dtypes on a hybrid model without skip layers, pads the drafter's sliding-window pages to the Mamba page (the fork's own comment: instead of wasting 16-token blocks inside 1.8 MB uniform pages). Without it the drafter's group carries a page and block size that do not line up with the rest, which is what prints the "Disabling fine-grained prefix-cache hits ... SlidingWindowManager" marker on every DFlash2 cell and never on MTP, and what makes the scheduler block the LCM of 128 and 448.

Restored against the port's installed file it is a seven-line insertion after the `skip_page_size_padded` line, and the attribute it reads, `cache_config.mamba_page_size_padded`, exists in 0.28.0 core. Two cells on the validated image with that patch applied at boot:

| cell | marker at boot | score | tool calls | replies capped / cut | turn two |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| KVarN + DFlash2 k=7, hunk restored | 0 | **16/18** | 72 | 1 / 0 | "NO-GO on that request. The pre-launch PAD is void: it was co..." |
| same, replicate | 0 | **11/18** | 85 | 3 / 0 | "Flight, FIDO. I can't uplink the pre-launch PAD. It was comp..." |
| baseline (three runs, above) | 1 | 6-9/18 | 16-28 | 0 / 9-11 | "duct Register Register ..." |

The repetition collapse is gone: no reply hits the cap, turn two is the same decision the 0.27.1 fork makes, and the tool-call count is back where KVarN with MTP sits. What remains is the second regression, the tool-loop capping (three of twelve turns on the replicate), which the drafter-free bf16 rows already isolated to the port's prefix caching on this hybrid and which this hunk does not address.

**The storage path (code review, round seven).** Without the hunk, `shared_page` is `None` for the drafter's sliding-window spec, so it starts at a 16-token block with a 65,536-byte page. The port's block-promotion patch then notices that page does not divide the Mamba page (1,827,840 bytes) and promotes the drafter to a 544-token block with a 2,228,224-byte page, which becomes the new maximum page for every group. Unification pads the target KVarN spec up to that maximum, 400,384 bytes more per manager block. Core allocation and group ownership use the unified page, but KVarN derives its block stride from its own packed shape, seventeen 128-token tiles per 2,176-token manager block. The allocator therefore advances by the promoted page while KVarN's packed view and its flush lifecycle advance by seventeen tiles; block ownership and byte ownership diverge, and a later allocation or flush overwrites bytes the target still addresses as another block. With the hunk, the drafter's page equals the Mamba page from the start, it is excluded from the promotion candidates, the maximum never grows, and the two views stay identical. The boot-time coordinator warning is a symptom of the same geometry, not the mechanism. The hunk is gated on the KVarN cache dtype, so it is a no-op for the int4, int8 and bf16 tiers, and a deployment with real skip layers keeps its own shared page.

Why prefix caching off still collapsed under the mechanism reading above: the damage is in the group geometry itself, present in the configuration whether or not a hit is ever served. The continuation tuples were a true description of the port's coarsened hits, not of the collapse.

### Everything else, falsified on the same cell (10:50Z)

Same mission, same card, KVarN k4v2 + DFlash2, validated image, the marker present at boot on every one of these:

| cut | what it tests | score | replies cut | turn two |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| round six candidate 1: the live-prefill guard (one line in the runner) | the final-prefill classifier | 8/18 | 8 of 12 | "duct Register ..." |
| round six candidate 2: no uniform captured-decode count (one line in the runner) | the FULL graph replay seam | 9/18 | 8 of 12 | "duct Register ..." |
| draft width 1 | dose-response | 2/18 | 12 of 12 | "duct Registerduct Register ..." |
| draft width 3 | dose-response | 4/18 | 12 of 12 | "duct Register ..." |
| `KVARN_SHARED_VERIFY=1` | the shared-dequant verify kernel | 7/18 | 9 of 12 | "duct Register ..." |
| round four: clear reused slots on re-staging | KVarN's continuation | 6/18 | 9 of 12 | "duct Register ..." |
| round three + four stacked | continuation through the slow path | 5/18 | 9 of 12 | "duct Register ..." |
| the rebased head's verify split-K line | formality (already split) | 7/18 | 10 of 12 | "duct Register ..." |

Every draft width collapses, and the narrowest widths collapse hardest, which fits a geometry defect that every verify step walks into rather than a width-dependent kernel path. The restored padding hunk is the only change on this image that removed the marker and the collapse. The maintainer's residue sweep did not run in this batch (the exec line lacked the MSYS path guard and the corpus path was rewritten); it is re-queued, unpatched and with the hunk, behind the new-head cells.

## Arc two: is the capping regression upstream, and are the workarounds still needed? (21:10Z)

**Is a fresh rebase cheap?** A hunk census of main's 0.27.1 series (29 patches plus the two KVarN patches) against a pristine 0.28.0 tree: 63 hunks fail. Thirty-three are the DFlash2 family, which 0.28.0 ships natively, so that is a real port and is the work #43 did. The other thirty fail across eight patches; the KVarN runner patch fails 8 of 13, and #43's regeneration recovered twelve and dropped one. A full re-port by hand is days and would mostly compare one re-contexting to another, so the rebase is scoped to whichever patch a cheaper cut implicates.

**The stock control.** Stock vLLM 0.28.0 (the official image) cannot load this checkpoint: AutoRound packs the embedding weights and only the fork's embedding patch reads them. That patch applies to 0.28.0 at one line of offset and touches nothing near scheduling, so the control is stock plus that one patch, launched with the fork's own arguments for the bf16 drafter-free cell, prefix caching on:

| tree | score | tool calls | capped / cut |
|---|---|---|---|
| stock 0.28.0 + embedding patch | **18/18** | 57 | **0 / 0** |
| the port (e34afaf), same cell | 15/18 | 172 | 9 / 0 |
| the 0.27.1 fork, same cell | 15/18 | | 0-1 / 0 |

The capping regression is not upstream. It is in the fork's 0.28 patch set or its launcher. Phase two bisects it on this cell: the fork's fixed image under stock's plain arguments (the launcher cut), then stock plus one subset of the fork's regenerated patches at a time: the KVarN runner and backend patches (the hash-unit logic applies to every hybrid), the Mamba checkpoint-order patch, the hybrid cudagraph and block-promotion patches, the sampler and knob patches.

**The two workaround commits.** Each reverted on top of the restored hunk, built, and run on the KVarN + DFlash2 cell with the marker check:

| tree | marker at boot | score | tool calls | capped / cut |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| fix branch (hunk restored) | 0 | 14/18 | 72 | 0 / 0 |
| fix branch minus "Fix v0.28 hybrid KVarN hash geometry" | 0 | 15/18 | 70 | 0 / 0 |
| fix branch minus "Skip unaligned sliding-window cache writes" | 0 | 12/18 | 57 | 1 / 0 |

Both revert cells boot without the marker and neither collapses; both scores sit inside the fix branch's own run-to-run range on this cell (11 to 16 of 18 across four runs). With the hunk restored the drafter's block is 128 tokens on the mamba page, which divides the hash unit and the scheduler block, so the conditions those two commits guard against (a sliding-window block that does not divide the hash unit, or a scheduler boundary finer than the block) are not met on this deployment. They read as dormant safety, not as wrong; whether to keep them is the author's call, and nothing here argues for removing them on a deployment with real skip layers or a different draft length. One run each.

### The capping bisect, round one (21:45Z)

Same cell throughout: bf16, speculation off, prefix caching on, the twelve-turn mission with the 900-token cap, the stock-style argument list (only what stock needs to serve the model with the fork's chat template and tool parser). One run per row; the rows with identical tool-call totals are identical transcripts.

| tree | arguments | score | tool calls | capped / cut |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| stock 0.28.0 + embedding patch | plain | 18/18 | 57 | 0 / 0 |
| stock + the KVarN backend and runner patches (hunk restored) | plain | 15/18 | **80** | **3** / 0 |
| stock + the Mamba checkpoint-order patch | plain | 15/18 | 57 | 1 / 0 |
| stock + the hybrid cudagraph and block-promotion patches | plain | 18/18 | 57 | 0 / 0 |
| stock + the sampler, sampler-prewarm, xgrammar and speed-knob patches | plain | 13/18 | 65 | 1 / 0 |
| the fix branch (every fork patch) | plain | 15/18 | **80** | **3** / 0 |
| the fix branch (every fork patch) | the fork's launcher | 15/18 | 172 | **9** / 0 |

Two contributions, cleanly separated. The whole patch set under plain arguments behaves exactly like stock plus the KVarN pair (80 tool calls, three capped turns, same score), so within the patches it is the KVarN backend and runner patches, whose hash-unit and coordinator logic applies to every hybrid whether or not KVarN is the cache dtype. The fork's launcher then takes three capped turns to nine on the same image: the arguments it adds over plain are async scheduling, the compilation config (custom rms_norm and silu_and_mul ops, a cudagraph capture size of 32), language-model-only, and the FlashInfer sampler switched off. Round two splits both: the KVarN pair into its two patches, and the launcher into those three groups.

### The capping bisect, round two (22:25Z): one hunk, and a caveat about the instrument

Round two split both round-one signals. All rows are the same cell (bf16, speculation off, prefix caching on, the mission at temperature 0) with the stock-style arguments unless marked.

| tree / knob | card | score | tool calls | capped / 12 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| stock + the KVarN backend patch only | 0 | 13/18 | 65 | 1 |
| stock + the runner patch minus its DFlash2 speculator hunks | 0 | 17/18 | 158 | 7 |
| stock + the runner patch's hash-geometry hunks only (kv_cache_utils, coordinator, single-type manager) | 0 | 13/18 | 65 | 1 |
| **stock + the runner patch's mamba_hybrid.py hunk only (one hunk)** | 0 | 15/18 | **172** | **9** |
| fix branch, plain + async scheduling | 1 | 15/18 | 57 | 1 |
| fix branch, plain + the compilation config | 1 | 15/18 | 55 | 1 |
| fix branch, plain + language-model-only + FlashInfer sampler off + api-server-count 1 | 1 | 15/18 | 144 | 7 |
| fix branch, plain + language-model-only | 1 / 0 | 15/18 / 18/18 | 55 / 50 | 1 / 0 |
| fix branch, plain + FlashInfer sampler off | 1 / 0 | 15/18 / 14/18 | 144 / 68 | 7 / 1 |
| fix branch, plain + api-server-count 1 (the default) | 1 | 16/18 | 151 | 7 |
| the port under its own launcher (earlier, three runs) | 0 | 15/18 | 172, 169, 172 | 9, 9, 9 |

**The caveat first.** This mission at temperature 0 is not run-to-run deterministic on this box (and only on this box; see below): the same image and arguments gave 80 tool calls with 3 capped and 151 with 7 on the same card, and the sampler switch gave 144/7 on one card and 68/1 on the other. The greedy trajectory has a handful of attractors (about 55 calls and 0-1 capped; about 65 and 1; about 80 and 3; about 145-160 and 7; 172 and 9), and any small numeric change can move a run between the mild ones. So the rows at 0-3 are noise, single rows at 7 are weak, and the metric has to be reported as a distribution. Replicates and a three-per-card distribution for stock and for the fork's launcher are running.

**The signal.** One hunk, applied alone to stock, lands on exactly the port's signature: 172 tool calls and 9 of 12 turns capped, the pair the port under its own launcher has produced three times out of three. It is the runner patch's change to `MambaHybridModelState.add_request`: on a prefix-cache resume in align mode it seeds the Mamba running-state block index as the resumed position divided by the Mamba spec's block size instead of `cache_config.block_size`. The 0.27.1 version carries the reason: with KVarN's explicit `--block-size 128` the two sizes diverged there and the core divisor indexed past the Mamba block table, an illegal memory access on resume. If 0.28.0 core counts that index in a different unit than 0.27.1 did, the same hunk now seeds the wrong Mamba state on every prefix resume for every hybrid with prefix caching on, drafter or not, which is the shape of the capping regression exactly: no crash, a model that resumes with a stale recurrent state and loops on tools, and prefix caching off removes it. Hypothesis until two things land: the code read of the unit (review round eight) and the fix branch rebuilt with that one hunk dropped, on the capping cell under the fork's launcher and on KVarN + DFlash2 with prefix caching for the crash the 0.27.1 comment describes. Both are queued.

### Round two closes: the Mamba seed hunk is not it, and the drift is this box (22:55Z)

**The candidate falsified.** The code review's round eight showed the hunk is numerically a no-op on the bf16 cell: in align mode 0.28.0 core sets the Mamba block size equal to the cache block size by construction (`platforms/interface.py`, the align branch after "Setting attention block size"), 400 tokens on this cell per the serve log, so the hunk's divisor and core's divisor seed the same Mamba column (7 at 2,917 computed tokens; the review's round eight put both at 448 and column 6, the same argument with the value the log shows). It stays necessary for KVarN's explicit 128-token block against a 2,176-token Mamba block, where dropping it would seed column 22 instead of 1 and bring back the illegal memory access the 0.27.1 comment describes; the round-eight draft reads the unit from `cache_config.mamba_block_size` instead, same value, cleaner. The measurement agreed: the fix branch rebuilt with that hunk dropped, under the fork's launcher, caps 9 of 12 with 172 tool calls, twice, identical to the branch with it.

**What the rows do say.** Under the fork's launcher the bf16 drafter-free cell lands on exactly 172 tool calls and 9 capped turns in six of six runs across two builds. Under stock-style arguments the same images wander (57/1, 80/3, 151/7), and stock lands 57/0 here. On the other seat's native RTX 3090 there is no wandering at all: stock and stock-plus-hunk gave 15/18, 86 tool calls, 0 capped, three runs identical to the digit and to 0.1 seconds of wall. So the run-to-run drift is a property of this box (WSL2, two cards that disagree with each other), not of the mission at temperature 0, and the earlier caption is corrected to say so. The batch-invariant mode 0.28.0 ships is refused for this model's GDN attention, so it cannot be used to pin this box.

**Where that leaves the capping regression.** No single fork patch reproduces it on stock; the launcher's argument set reproduces it every time. The three arguments the launcher adds over plain that are not 0.28.0 defaults: the custom rms_norm and silu_and_mul ops, a cudagraph capture size of 32, and language-model-only (async scheduling is the 0.28.0 default and was never a difference; the FlashInfer sampler switch is hardcoded in the launcher). Next cut, both boxes: the launcher with one of those removed per cell, and on the native box first, where a flip from a deterministic baseline is the result that counts.

### The second box does not cap at all (23:20Z)

The other seat ran the fork's own launcher on its validation tree with the padding hunk, the same cell (bf16, speculation off, prefix caching on), natively on the RTX 3090: 16/18, 69 tool calls, 0 capped, twice, identical. Its stock-argument runs were 15/18, 86 tool calls, 0 capped, four times identical. Every configuration on that box lands on a stable good trajectory. On this box the same launcher configuration lands at 172 tool calls and 9 capped in seven of nine runs, 144 and 7 once, 55 and 1 once, and the same images under plain arguments wander between 57/1 and 151/7, while 0.27.1 under the same launcher has been clean in every run here today and stock 0.28.0 was clean in its one run so far.

**Configuration held fixed (23:35Z).** The other seat diffed the resolved argument line of its fork-launcher cell against this box's (the serve log's non-default args, 53 fields): identical on every field that matters, model, template, tool parser, max length 57,344, four sequences, bf16 KV, align mode, language-model-only, async scheduling, the custom ops, capture size 32. Same engine version, equivalent tree. Theirs 69 tool calls and 0 capped twice; here 172 and 9 in seven of nine. The stack in the containers here is the same across the three images apart from vLLM itself: flashinfer-python 0.6.16.post3, torch 2.13.0, triton 3.7.1 on the port, on stock and on the 0.27.1 image alike. What differs between the two boxes is hardware (RTX 4090 against RTX 3090), WSL2 against native Linux, container against bare virtualenv, and nothing in the package stack: the native venv reports the same vLLM 0.28.0, flashinfer-python 0.6.16.post3, torch 2.13.0 and triton 3.7.1, with no separate flash-attn wheel on either side (its FlashInfer version-check override is about the cubin package pairing, not a different kernel set). The only launcher environment variable on both sides is the FlashInfer sampler switch, equal on both; an earlier note here naming a second variable came from a grep that matched a core log message, and is withdrawn.

So the capping regression is not selected by the launcher's arguments and not caused by any one fork patch. It is an interaction between the port's stack and this environment, WSL2 on RTX 4090s, that the native RTX 3090 does not show at all, and it is the same shape as the throughput scatter earlier today on the same box. What is still open is why the port's stack drifts here when 0.27.1 does not: the per-card distributions for stock and for the port (three samples each per card) and the Marlin GEMM subset are queued, the other seat is running this box's exact resolved configuration natively, and the launcher-knob cells still run here for the distribution table.

### Stock 0.28.0 caps on this box too (23:50Z)

The second stock run (0.28.0 plus only the embedding patch, plain arguments, GPU 1): 15/18, 144 tool calls, 7 of 12 capped, after 57 and 0 in the first. So the bad trajectory is reachable by stock 0.28.0 itself on this box, and the fork's port is not worse than stock here. The native box shows nothing on stock or on the port. The fork's 0.27.1 has been clean here in every run today. The one split left is box against version: stock vLLM 0.27.1, the official image plus the same embedding patch, is queued here three times. If it drifts as well, the capping is this box's numerics meeting a greedy mission; if it stays clean, the drift arrived with 0.28.0 core on this environment. Either outcome leaves the fork's port and #43 out of it. The launcher-knob cells were dropped from the queue as no longer worth the card; the remaining stock and port samples, the stock 0.27.1 runs and the Marlin subset finish the distribution table.

## The capping regression, concluded as a distribution (23:45Z)

**Naming the two failures.** The *runaway* is the KVarN + DFlash2 repetition collapse ("duct Register ..." to the length cap), fixed by the hunk. The *poll loop* is this one: in a capped turn the controller alternates `telemetry` and `request_evaluation` calls, dozens of pairs in a row, or reads telemetry thirty-odd times consecutively, and never commits to a decision until the harness's per-turn tool-call limit ends the turn. The harness allows sixteen tool iterations per turn; a capped turn has used all sixteen and produced no final text at all (reply length zero, finish `max_iters`), so the score counts it as a turn with no decision. The calls inside it are coherent and well formed: telemetry with three distinct argument sets and nine distinct results, alternating with an evaluation request whose result is always the same, "evaluation started; engineering needs one revolution of data; result available next rev; cleared: false". The model keeps asking for the clearance the scenario withholds until the next revolution instead of answering that it is holding until then. Context is not the limit: a fully capped run reaches 18,906 prompt tokens by turn twelve against a 57,344-token window, and a clean run 9,667. The metric is *capped turns*, turns that ended at the sixteen-iteration limit. The box-dependent variation in how often a configuration falls into the poll loop is the *drift*.

Every run of the bf16, speculation-off, prefix-caching-on mission on this box, grouped by what was serving. Thinking off, temperature 0, the 900-token reply cap; a turn is "capped" when the tool loop hits its iteration limit.

<!-- capdist-start -->
| configuration (bf16, speculation off, prefix caching on, this box) | runs | capped turns per run | tool calls per run |
|---|---|---|---|
| stock vLLM 0.27.1 + embedding patch, plain arguments | 3 | 1, 1, 7 (1 of 3 at 7 or more) | 55, 65, 151 |
| stock vLLM 0.28.0 + embedding patch, plain arguments | 8 | 7, 1, 1, 7, 1, 1, 1, 0 (2 of 8 at 7 or more) | 144, 65, 68, 144, 57, 41, 57, 57 |
| stock 0.28.0 + one fork subset, plain arguments | 14 | 1, 7, 1, 9, 7, 3, 7, 7, 7, 1, 3, 1, 0, 1 (6 of 14 at 7 or more) | 65, 158, 65, 172, 144, 80, 158, 144, 151, 68, 80, 57, 57, 65 |
| fork 0.28 (fix branch), plain arguments | 12 | 1, 7, 1, 1, 7, 7, 9, 7, 1, 1, 0, 3 (5 of 12 at 7 or more) | 57, 144, 55, 55, 144, 151, 166, 144, 57, 68, 50, 80 |
| fork 0.28 (fix branch), the fork's launcher | 9 | 9, 7, 1, 9, 9, 9, 9, 9, 9 (8 of 9 at 7 or more) | 172, 144, 55, 172, 172, 172, 172, 172, 172 |
| fork 0.27.1 (main), the fork's launcher | 3 | 2, 1, 2 (0 of 3 at 7 or more) | 75, 38, 81 |
<!-- capdist-end -->

~~On the native RTX 3090 (the other seat, seven cells across four configurations, stock 0.28.0 with and without the Mamba hunk, the fork's launcher on the fix tree with and without an extra knob): zero capped turns in every run, tool calls 64 to 86, scores 14 to 16 of 18, and every configuration run twice was identical to the digit.~~ *(Struck 04:10Z, September 4: the native box's capped counts below were recomputed; see "The native box's counts, recomputed".)*

**What this says.** The capping is not a defect in the fork's port, not a defect in vLLM 0.28.0, and not selected by any single patch or argument. On this box (WSL2, two RTX 4090s) the greedy mission has a small set of attractor trajectories, and every stack, stock 0.27.1 included, falls into the bad one in a third to a half of its runs under plain arguments. The fork's launcher argument set on the 0.28 branch raises that to nearly every run here, which is the "regression" the earlier sections chased; the same launcher on the fork's 0.27.1 lands in the good regime in three of three (2, 1, 2 capped), so on this box the launcher configuration does separate the two versions even though both stock versions drift under plain arguments. That makes a per-commit bisect of vLLM between the tags under the launcher configuration on this box a real option (the readout is reproducible here, nightly wheels exist per commit, about forty minutes per point), with the caveat that it would locate the commit that moves this box's odds, not a defect visible on native hardware. On native hardware the attractor is never reached. The batch-invariant mode 0.28.0 ships cannot be applied to this model's GDN attention, so this box cannot be pinned. For the flightbench instrument the consequence is plain: a single greedy mission is not a capping detector on this box, and the metric has to be reported as a distribution over runs, or measured with sampling over seeds.

**What it does not say.** That the port is safe from tool-loop capping in production on this hardware: on this box the 0.28 branch under the fork's launcher landed in the bad regime in eight of nine runs on this cell. The production tier is int4 with DFlash2, a different cell whose rows today were 0 to 1 capped; its distribution under the launcher on both versions is being measured now, five runs on the fix branch and three on 0.27.1, and that is the number the decision to move the int4 server rests on.

He answered with the text: his broken answers are coherent refusals in Danish ("I cannot reproduce the first 60 lines verbatim, as that would be a long direct quote of the supplied material," followed by an offer to summarise), zero document coverage, wording that changes between restarts, and he agrees the 127/128 is not a hunk verdict on his box and that the twelve-turn mission is the discriminator. ### The container layer, separated from the box (03:40Z, September 4)

The other seat ran the capping cell inside containers on its native RTX 3090, same card, beside its bare-venv rows: stock 0.28.0 plus the embedding patch (image 958a9bfa9851, the Dockerfile above verbatim) and the fork's launcher on its tree with the hunk (image 581a45dae301), three runs each.

| arm, native RTX 3090 | runs | tool calls | capped |
|---|---|---|---|
| bare venv, stock-style arguments | 4 | 86, 86, 86, 86 | 0, 0, 0, 0 |
| container, stock-style arguments | 3 | 189, 78, 74 | 0, 0, 0 |

Two things fall out, pointing opposite ways. The container layer alone reproduces the run-to-run wandering on native hardware: bare, four runs identical to the digit; containerised, a spread of 115 tool calls with the same arguments. So the non-determinism is not WSL2's and not the 4090's; a container is sufficient for it, and the caption "not run-to-run deterministic on this box" narrows to "in containers". And the container layer does not produce the poll loop: zero capped turns in all six container cells, on both images; that box has now run thirteen cells, bare and containerised, stock and fork, and never capped once. The poll loop therefore belongs to what remains on this box after containers are excluded: WSL2, or the RTX 4090s, or both, which is exactly the pair the native-Linux boot on this hardware separates. One more observation from the native rows, worth a replicate here before it is a claim: inside the container the stock arguments swing 74 to 189 while the fork's launcher arguments hold 64 to 71, so the launcher's argument set may stabilise the trajectory rather than select one; on this box the trajectory it stabilises on is the bad one.

The other seat's "capped" column had counted the token-length finish, not the sixteen-iteration finish that this document's metric is. Recomputed from the same traces, nothing re-run, on the native RTX 3090:

| arm | runs | score | tool calls | capped (sixteen-iteration finish) |
