## ff7a: the first-config kernels are twice as slow at the default on card 0 (2026-09-05, 08:50Z)

The first clean wall clock of the arc, card 1 idle throughout and card 0 sampled at 8 lanes, Gen4, 2730 to 2745 MHz: width 7 with VLLM_TRITON_FORCE_FIRST_CONFIG=1 (the flag confirmed installed in the log, async on, engine line num_speculative_tokens 7) runs at 46.9 ms per step in steady state (46.5 to 47.3 across rows) against 23.3 to 23.4 for the autotuned w7 and blk7. Tokens per step 3.701 on 30 rows. The registered hope for this flag, a determinism fix that was also a speed-up, is dead on both counts: the card-1 pair diverged more than autotuned boots, and on card 0 the first-listed configuration is the slow one for at least one of the six kernels. The card-1 observation that ff1 ran 15 percent faster than its autotuned twin is withdrawn as a contaminated clock: every card-1 cell ran beside a busy card 0, and this is the first force-first-config boot measured under the one-engine rule. The autotuner is doing its job on this card; the per-boot variance it was suspected of causing is not its doing. [Withdrawn 2026-09-05: the neighbour, lane and contamination attributions in this paragraph did not survive; see the section beginning "First, the one-engine-per-host rule loses its evidence" and the corrections from the second fresh-eyes review at the end of this record.]

VLLM_TRITON_FORCE_FIRST_CONFIG=1 produced empty completions on 2 of 32 rows at the launcher default: seed 2 and seed 3 on prompt 5 (the schema prompt) returned a single token, an end of sequence, in 0.1 seconds, where the autotuned boots return 1024 tokens on the same seeds. That is a correctness statement, not a timing one: the first-listed kernel configuration is numerically different enough to flip a token that ends generation, on a flag someone would reach for precisely when chasing nondeterminism. It is the reason to reject the flag that would matter to a reader who does not care about speed, and it sits beside the speed number for that reason. The two rows are excluded from the means above (30 rows), and the same blindness noted earlier in the arc applies from the other end: a one-token row has near-meaningless counters and would drag an aggregate without looking like an outlier in tokens per step.

ff7b, the second force-first-config boot at the default on card 0 (2026-09-05, 09:10Z): 45.0 ms per step steady (the 2x confirmed on a second clean boot), tokens per step 3.650, and 4 of 30 rows identical to ff7a, so the flag's boots diverge on card 0 as they did on card 1 (26 of 30 rows apart against 0 of 32 for w7 and std7 under the autotuner). The two empty completions recur exactly: seed 2 and seed 3 on prompt 5 return a single token in both boots. The empty completion is therefore a deterministic property of the first-listed kernel configuration on this cohort, not a one-off draw, which makes the correctness line above firmer than it was: under that flag, this model ends prompt 5 on its first token for two of four seeds, every time.

What the empty completion is, registered before the classifying cells run (2026-09-05, 09:25Z). At the model's sampling (temperature 1.0, top-k 20) an end-of-sequence token drawn first is a legitimate sample whenever EOS sits in the top twenty for that prompt, so a kernel configuration whose numerics move a seeded draw across that boundary is a caution about the flag, not a defect in it. If greedy decoding under the same flag also ends prompt 5 on its first token, the configuration makes EOS the argmax for an ordinary prompt, and that is a numerics defect with a one-line reproduction. Two cells decide it: ffg7 here (width 7, VLLM_TRITON_FORCE_FIRST_CONFIG=1, temperature 0, text saved, queued at the end of the card-0 queue), and the same pair on the native 3090 (sampled with four seeds, then greedy), which corroborates across cards. The flag is upstream vLLM's (envs.py and triton_utils/force_first_config.py), so if greedy confirms the defect the report belongs to vllm-project with a one-line pointer on the fork's thread; if it does not, the caution stays in this document and the draft note. Either way it is Michael's pen, and it does not outrank the arc's own answer to his question; it is the one correctness item the arc produced, and correctness items travel on their own merits.

Cross-card corroboration, exact (2026-09-05, 09:35Z). The native 3090, no container, no WSL2, ran the flag at the default width with seeds 1 to 4: the one-token rows are prompt 5 at seed 2 and prompt 5 at seed 3, the same two, with seeds 1 and 4 on that prompt running to 1024 tokens and 26 of 32 rows full length (four ordinary early stops elsewhere). One comparison removes the four things the night could not separate: card numerics, the boot state, WSL2 and the image. Whatever it is, it lives in the kernel configuration the flag selects and it is portable. The control is already in hand: that box's earlier force-first-config run at seeds 1000 plus the prompt index produced no empty rows at all, prompt 5 included, so the flag does not empty prompt 5 as such; it empties prompt 5 at these two seeds, which is the shape the classifier predicts if EOS has been moved near the top of the first-token distribution rather than to it. Two boxes agreeing on which seeds hit it says the distribution under the flag is the same on both, as it should be; it says nothing yet about whether that distribution is wrong. The greedy pass decides, on both boxes, and the conclusion is held until both are in.

The rows add a fact about the instrument: the empty rows report zero drafts, zero accepted, zero round. They contribute nothing to the acceptance counters, so they are invisible in tokens per step and in per-position acceptance and drag nothing; they show only in a completion-length column. Every acceptance-based comparison in this arc would have passed over them. That is the third way these counters have been found blind to output quality tonight, after repetition loops scoring 80 percent acceptance and one-token rows in a wall-clock mean, and the completion length now joins distinct-word and gzip ratios as a column every cell reports and every reading checks.

Greedy under the flag on the native 3090 (2026-09-05, 09:55Z): every prompt runs to a full answer (seven at 1024 tokens, prompt 7 complete at 806), prompt 5 at 1024, no one-token row. EOS is not the argmax for prompt 5 under the first-listed kernel configuration; the sampled one-token rows are legitimate draws with EOS inside the top twenty, and the flag moved a seeded draw across a boundary rather than putting EOS on top. By the classifier registered before the cells ran, that is a caution about the configuration and not a correctness defect, and it does not warrant an upstream report on this evidence. Said plainly because the pull was the other way: the exact cross-card agreement on which two seeds land on the boundary is the most striking cross-box agreement of the arc, and it is still not evidence of a bug, only that the distribution under the flag is the same on both cards, as it should be. What survives is a third the size and real: VLLM_TRITON_FORCE_FIRST_CONFIG=1 runs about twice as slow at the default width on the 4090, is not a determinism fix (boots under it diverge more, not less), and changes the output distribution enough to flip which seeds terminate immediately, reproducibly and identically on two cards, which is worth one line beside the speed number for anyone reaching for the flag to make behaviour more predictable. ffg7 here still runs: if this box's greedy pass ends prompt 5 on its first token while the 3090's does not, the two cards disagree at the argmax under the same configuration, a stronger and opposite statement, and one clean pass settles it either way. The empty rows' invisibility to every acceptance statistic stays true whatever the final reading.

## The kernel read, and the one cell that isolates qmax (2026-09-05, 13:05Z)

The split-KV verify attention (vllm/v1/attention/ops/spec_decode_attn.py, the same file in the image and on the native box, sha256 beginning 53ee3e78, no patched original beside it) plans its tiles on the actual maximum query length of the step (line 217, the plan function at 188), not on the query maximum, so std1 at round 7.1 and t7b at round 7.0 get the same block size, the same tiles and the same warp count, and a tile or sixteen-query shape boundary is excluded by source. The query maximum enters in two places, both at setup: the partial buffers are sized linearly in it (line 183), and it is a compile-time constant in both kernels (lines 67 and 147), so a different maximum compiles a different kernel. t8 at qmax 9 lands at 23.2, not partway to 56, which argues against the linear half and leaves the compilation: a register or scheduling threshold crossed somewhere above 9, discrete, as the data demands. The launcher exports VLLM_SPEC_DECODE_ATTN_QMAX from an inherited value when one is set (line 223), and the kernel asserts that the step's query length does not exceed it, so raising it above the need is safe while lowering it is not. The cell that settles it is one boot, q16_7: DFLASH_TOKENS=7 with the query maximum set to 16, identical width, positions per step and tile plan, only the maximum changed. Registered: near 56 means the query maximum alone is the mechanism, the kernel compiled at that constant on this card; near 23 exonerates it and t11 behind it brackets the width step instead. The native box runs the same cell as its control, expected flat, since its width-15 path costs 2 percent and removing the kernel there changes nothing. The two-cell width bracket is replaced by this; t13 is dropped unrun.

**The graphs-off yardstick, native.** CUDA graphs off at width 7 on the 3090: 50.5 against 25.1 ms per step, 2.01x. Kernel launches are not free on bare metal either, so the framing that passthrough multiplies launch cost while native launches are free is withdrawn by its author and here: the native box is as launch-sensitive in relative terms. It does not revive launch-boundness as the width mechanism (23 against 56 at matched positions, and now the identical tile plan, stand without reference to launches); it gives cgn7a and cgn7b a yardstick: a ratio near 2x against their side's base means launch overhead is proportionally the same on both hosts and carries none of the width story; far above 2x means passthrough multiplies launches, a separate line, still not the mechanism.

## ff15, first rows: under a fixed kernel configuration the width costs almost nothing (2026-09-05, 13:15Z)

| configured width | first-listed kernel config | autotuned |
|---|---|---|
| 7 | 45 to 47 | 23 or 45 |
| 15 | 49.5 | 56.5 |

Under one fixed kernel configuration, going from width 7 to width 15 costs about 4 ms per step, not 33. The 2.4x "width penalty" is therefore the autotuner's choice: at width 7 the race lands on a fast configuration on six boots of ten and on the slow side on four; at width 15 it landed on a configuration slower than the first-listed one on five of five boots. The bimodality at the default and the width-15 cost are one phenomenon, the Triton autotune race on this card choosing slow winners for at least one of the chunked GDN kernels, often at width 7 and always at width 15, which is issue 75's report with a mechanism and a price. The native 3090 shows none of it (nine unimodal boots, forced first config indistinguishable from autotuned, width 15 at 2 percent), so the race's outcome is card-specific, whether because the candidate that wins here is bad on this silicon or because timing under this host misleads the race. Registered before r7a to r7d land: on the critical kernel, the slow boots' winner is the first-listed configuration or one of its speed class, and the fast boots' winner is a different configuration; if fast and slow boots share every winner, the race is exonerated after all and the per-process GPU path returns as the candidate. q16_7 (the query maximum alone) and sa0_15 (the split-KV verify kernel off) still run and now read as the alternatives to this: if either collapses the width-15 cost toward 23 on its own, the reading above is wrong about which kernel.

The other seat amends its own "set by the configured maximum" claim in place: under one fixed kernel configuration the width costs about 4 ms on both cards (this card 45 to 47 at width 7 against 49.5 at 15, that card 25.4 against 25.5), so the configured width matters here only because it changes what the autotuner picks, not because wider buffers or a differently compiled kernel are slower. Lined up under the same fixed configuration this card is about 1.8x the native one at both widths, ordinary hardware difference plus a first-listed configuration that suits a 3090 better than a 4090. The part that is not ordinary, and the candidate headline: at width 15 this card's autotuner picks a winner slower than the first-listed configuration five times of five (56.5 against 49.5), and at width 7 it does so four times of ten; the native box never does (seven autotuned boots at 24.7 to 25.5 against forced-first at 25.2 and 25.5, at worst equal), with the caveat that a 5 percent regression would hide inside its spread while a 2x could not. An autotuner that deterministically selects worse than its own first entry is a defect in its own right, independent of which kernel and of the width story, and it is the strongest cross-box asymmetry of the night. r7a to r7d name the kernel or exonerate the race; q16_7 here is expected flat like the native control.

For the maintainer, the sentence is already a sentence: on a 4090 under WSL2, reproduction mode's step cost is the split-KV verify attention at a query maximum of 16, and turning that kernel off (SPEC_ATTN=0) returns the step to the default's cost with the reproduction-mode acceptance intact.

Registered before more greedy boots run: a greedy trio at the default on card 1 (g7t1 to g7t3, winners printed, queued after the cache-off pairs) all at the fast side means the bimodality lives in the sampled path; a split means it does not. So the kernel configuration moves the greedy trajectory by about as much as the card does, and the two do not cancel: the cross-card divergence is not simply the GDN configuration, and a same-config greedy comparison across cards remains a comparison of two numerics, not one.

## cgn7b lands slow, and its startup capture says why it differs from cgn7a (2026-09-05, 17:35Z)

The second graphs-off boot at the default reads 46.5 ms per step steady (46.1 to 47.6) against cgn7a's 33.7: the graphs-off pair is itself two-sided, and the slow side under no graphs at all (46.5) sits where the slow side with graphs sits (45), so whatever the slow side is, it is not about graphs. This time both boots carry their startup lines to the end, and they differ in one thing. cgn7a compiled from scratch: "Dynamo bytecode transform time: 12.11 s", "Compiling a graph for compile range (1, 2048) takes 57.68 s", the six chunked GDN kernels raced (110 autotune lines, winners chosen), and "saved AOT compiled function to .../torch_aot_compile/6cfcec20...". cgn7b loaded exactly that artefact: "Directly load AOT compilation from path .../6cfcec20...", no compile, no race. Engine configuration identical (the non-default args lines match). The boot that compiled ran fast; the boot that loaded what it had compiled ran slow.

The registered artefact comparison therefore separates the sides, in the registered direction (a slow boot loading a different artefact set from the fast boots), with three caveats stated before anyone leans on it. First, this pair is graphs-off; the default's fast boots r7b, r7c and r7d also loaded artefacts directly (three each, the same three) and were fast, so "loaded means slow" is false as a rule. Second, cgn7b loaded one artefact where the default boots load three, the count threadchip flagged as a distinct mechanism (a graph missing the cache). Third, std7 and a7off2, the two slow boots at graphs-on configurations, have no startup capture, so whether they loaded, compiled, or loaded a different set is unknown. What the pair does establish is that the cache can hand a boot a slower version of what another boot just ran fast, on this host, and that the difference between a compiling boot and a loading boot is not visible to the counters (tokens per step 3.736 against 3.750).

The direct test is cheap and registered before it runs: two default boots with the compile cache disabled (VLLM_DISABLE_COMPILE_CACHE=1, so each compiles and races from scratch), winners printed, startup lines captured, beside the four warm-loaded default boots already in hand. Cold boots all fast means the loaded artefact is the slow side's carrier, which would make issue 75's scatter (measured with the cache disabled, where every boot re-races) and this arc's scatter (measured with it warm, where a loaded artefact can be slow) two faces of the same cache; a cold boot that lands slow means the compile itself can produce a slow build and the cache only replays it. Either reading puts the mechanism in what the compile cache stores and how a loaded artefact's Triton kernels are configured, which is the maintainer's territory and issue 75's thread.

Width 7 with VLLM_SPEC_DECODE_ATTN_QMAX=16 (engine line num_speculative_tokens 7, the RESOLVED line QMAX 16; identical width, identical positions per step, round 7.000, identical tile plan by source): 63.2 to 64.5 ms per step on the first four rows, against 23.2 for the same configuration at the maximum of 8. The registered prediction (slow, in the 45 to 56 band; 23 would have killed the line) holds in direction and overshoots in size: the constant alone costs 2.7x at width 7, more than the whole width-15 configuration costs at its adaptive round (56.5), which fits a kernel compiled for sixteen query slots and paying for them on every step whatever the step uses. With sa0_15 (the kernel off at width 15, 23.6) and the native controls (the kernel off or on, the maximum 8 or 16, all inside a 0.8 ms band on the 3090), the width-15 finding is complete and one sentence long: on a 4090 under WSL2, the fork's split-KV Triton verify attention compiled with a query maximum of 16 runs 2.4 to 2.7x slow, on a 3090 it costs nothing, and reproduction mode (DFLASH_TOKENS=15) is where the launcher sets that maximum. The held clause is released in the note, the write-up and the board: "at a query maximum of 16" is the operative part. What a user on this card can do today: SPEC_ATTN=0 at width 15 (stock attention for the verify, the reproduction-mode acceptance intact, 23.6 ms per step); what the kernel's author can do is look at the QMAX constant's effect on its compilation for sm89, which is one kernel and one constant. The remaining rows and the fallback bracket (t11) follow; t11 now reads only as a second point on the same curve.

The unification proposed from the other box, with the arithmetic that does not yet close (17:58Z). Every slow boot of the arc sits at the forced-first-configuration speed: ff7a 47.0 and ff7b 45.0 (forced), std7 45.0, a7off2 44.9 and cgn7b 46.5 (loaded artefacts), a range of 44.9 to 47.0 with the two forced boots inside the range of the three that got there by themselves. Read naively, the slow mode is the first-configuration mode: a boot is slow when it ends up running the first-listed Triton configurations, whether forced or because the artefact it loaded carried them, and std7, a7off2 and cgn7b are one mystery, issue 75's. cgn7b sharpens it: the artefact it loaded was saved by cgn7a, which had raced and run fast, so a loaded artefact does not carry the winners the compiling boot raced for; its kernels run at the first-listed configuration. What does not close: graph capture is worth 10.5 ms on fast kernels (33.7 against 23.2) and 1.5 ms on slow ones (46.5 against 45.0), where a per-launch overhead should be the same either way, nine milliseconds unaccounted for. Three ways out, distinguishable: the slow side is a family of speeds hiding in 44.9 to 47.0; the slow kernels overlap launch overhead in their gaps, so capture and kernel choice are not additive (which ff15 at 49.5 against std1 at 56.5 already hinted); or std7 is a different failure landing near 45 by coincidence. The cold pair separates the first from the third if its winners are printed: a cold boot landing slow with winners matching the first-listed configurations makes the unification hold and the gap a physics question; a cold slow boot with different winners breaks it. The winner script now prints the first-listed configuration per kernel beside each boot's winner for exactly that comparison. The native side stays a null and is not dressed up: two cold boots inside the warm band, forced-first indistinguishable from autotuned, no slow build, no slow load, no first-configuration penalty on that card.

Scoring the q16_7 prediction honestly, and one sentence corrected (2026-09-05, 18:15Z). The registered band was 45 to 56 with 23 as the falsifier; the cell read 63.2 to 64.5. Direction correct, range wrong, recorded as such by its author: the band had been anchored on the width-15 configuration's 56.5 as a ceiling, with no mechanism behind the anchoring, and the two configurations were never comparable, because the launcher rule (LOOKUP=1 and DRAFT_TOKENS above 7 turns async scheduling off) fires for std1 and not for q16_7. q16_7 is async on; std1 is synchronous. The isolation itself is untouched: q16_7 against t7b is width 7 both sides, async on both sides, round 7.000 both sides, only the query maximum differing, so 63 against 23.2 isolates the constant exactly. The sentence above that says the constant alone costs more than the whole width-15 configuration crossed the async boundary and is withdrawn: the constant costs 2.7x on the async path, and the width-15 configuration's 56.5 is the same constant on the synchronous path with the adaptive length working, which makes reproduction mode the cheaper of the two ways to hold the maximum at 16, and the launcher's coupling a saving for the third time in this arc. The compounding is worth one line for the maintainer: on a 4090 under WSL2 the QMAX-16 kernel and async scheduling are both expensive and they stack (23.2 at QMAX 8 async, 56.5 at QMAX 16 synchronous, 63 to 64.5 at QMAX 16 async), so a user who raised DFLASH_TOKENS above 7 and forced async back on would land worst of all, and the coupling is what stops them. The native matched pair is the cleanest single sentence of the finding: QMAX 16 against 8 at width 7, both async on, 25.0 against 25.1 on the 3090, the same isolation that costs this card 2.7x.

q16_7 complete (2026-09-05, 18:35Z): 32 rows, 63.5 ms per step steady (63.2 to 64.1), tokens per step 3.798, 59.7 tok/s, async_scheduling True on the engine line, the trajectory a replay of the fast default boots in tokens per step. The isolation stands as stated: only the query maximum differed from t7b, and it cost 2.7x on this card.

t11 (2026-09-05, 18:55Z): width 11, so a query maximum of 12, lookup on and synchronous by the rule (engine line async False): 23.4 ms per step steady (23.0 to 28.6), tokens per step 3.798, round 7.105, 160.3 tok/s. Fast. The query-maximum curve on this card now reads 23.2 at 8, 23.2 at 9, 23.4 at 12, 56.5 at 16 (synchronous, width 15) and 63.5 at 16 (async, width 7): a step between 13 and 16, not a ramp, consistent with a compile-time boundary the kernel crosses at or near sixteen slots (a register or shared-memory threshold in the compiled kernel at that constant), which is one constant on one architecture for the kernel author. sa0_7a is running; the cold pair follows.

## cold7a: a cold compile lands at a third level (2026-09-05, about 10:27Z; the stamp first written here, 10:52Z, was again an estimate and not a clock reading, corrected at 10:36Z)

cold7a (the launcher default with VLLM_DISABLE_COMPILE_CACHE=1; RESOLVED compiled=3 aot_loaded=0, no "saved AOT compiled function" line, so the graphs were compiled in process and nothing was written or loaded; the six chunked GDN kernels raced, 100 autotune lines, 4 out-of-resource failures as in every race): 28.0 ms per step steady (27.7 to 28.7), tokens per step 3.720 against the default trajectory's 3.798, round 7.000, 131.8 tok/s, three completions under 1024. The trajectory is 0 of 32 identical with the default (per-position 198, 143, 108, 82, 63, 50, 39 against 201, 146, 110, 85, 67, 54, 43), which is what a re-raced kernel set should give if the kernel configuration is the third axis (the force-first boots diverged the same way); the winners differ across the three cold-raced boots in hand (cold7a, cgn7a, t8: chunk_fwd_kernel_o at BK 64 stages 2, BK 64 stages 3, BK 32 stages 3; the cumsum kernel at 2, 8 and 4 warps; the merge kernel at 2 and 4 warps), so the race does not converge on one set on this card, and only one kernel of six lands on its first-listed configuration in each. What 28.0 is beside 23.2 is not settled by one boot: t8 also raced its kernels and ran 23.2, but t8 loaded the torch.compile artefact and raced only the Triton kernels, where cold7a had no artefact at all, so the two differ in the compiled graph as well as the winners. cold7b (up 10:51Z) is the same configuration again: a second 28 makes the disabled cache a stable cost of about 5 ms on this card and says the cold compile does not produce the 45 side (the loaded artefact remains its only carrier in hand); a 45 says the compile can; a 23 says the 28 was the winners.

12. (about 10:33Z; first stamped 11:20Z, an estimate, corrected; threadchip on the capture table) The isolation the cold pair was meant to give is already in the table, and cold7b does not add it. Sorted by what each boot did: the boots that compiled fresh and raced with the cache enabled ran 23.4 (t11, width 11) and 23.6 (sa0_7a, width 7 with the verify kernel off, measured at +0.4 ms over the default), and cgn7a at 33.7 with graphs off, whose gap is capture; cold7a did the same two things with the flag and ran 28.0. So a fresh compile and a race are held constant across that comparison, and with the one stated assumption (the +0.4 ms adjustment for sa0_7a, so a width-7 default fresh-compiled raced boot projects to about 23.2, which is also the warm default), cold7a's excess of +4.8 ms is attributable to what VLLM_DISABLE_COMPILE_CACHE=1 does beyond forcing the race: the winner set it happened to land on (cold7a's differs from t11's on every kernel listed) or an in-process cache the flag also disables. cold7b varies nothing, so its reading is re-registered here before it lands, conditional on its own winner set: a different winner set at 23 says the 28 was cold7a's winners; a different winner set at 28 says the flag; the same winner set at 28 is ambiguous; the same set at 23 is noise to be counted. The cell that tests the winners without the flag is a pin: reorder each chunked GDN kernel's autotune configuration list so cold7a's winner is first and boot with VLLM_TRITON_FORCE_FIRST_CONFIG=1 on a normal cache; a 28 makes the winners the whole 4.8 ms. That is a patch to six configuration lists inside the image rather than an environment variable, registered as a candidate behind the queue and not run. The line that stands on its own, measured and needing no cross-box support: the race does not converge on this card (three cold races, three winner sets, one of six kernels on its first-listed configuration each time), and the native card cannot speak to it because nothing there re-races.

## cold7b: a fourth level, and half of cold7a's trajectory (2026-09-05, 10:35Z, stamped by the shell clock; from here every stamp in this record is written by date -u at the moment of the append, because the four stamps above were estimates even after the erratum said they would not be)

cold7b (the same configuration as cold7a: the launcher default with VLLM_DISABLE_COMPILE_CACHE=1; RESOLVED compiled=3 aot_loaded=0, raced, nothing saved): 33.8 ms per step steady (33.4 to 34.5), tokens per step 3.810, round 7.000, 112.0 tok/s, three completions under 1024. Its winner set differs from cold7a's on four kernels of six (chunk_fwd_kernel_o stages 3 against 2, the cumsum kernel at 4 warps against 2, chunk_scaled_dot_kkt at BK 64 stages 3 against BK 32 stages 4, recompute_w_u at 4 warps against 8; the two kernels they share are the blockdim64 kernel, on its first-listed configuration in both, and the merge kernel), and its trajectory is 16 of 32 rows identical with cold7a and 0 of 32 with the default. By the reading registered in item 12 this is "a different winner set at neither 23 nor 28": the two boots of one configuration under the flag differ by 5.8 ms per step with the winner set the only thing known to differ between them, so on this card the winner set a race lands on moves the step cost by at least that much between boots, and the flag's own fixed cost, if it has one, stays unresolved beneath it. The four raced boots at graphs on now span 23.4, 23.6, 28.0 and 33.8 with four winner sets, the warm loads that never race sit at 23.2 (r7a to r7d, t7b to t7d) or 45 (std7, cgn7b), and the first-listed set sits at 45 to 47. That is the strongest form of the arc's kernel-configuration finding for this card: the step cost of the chunked GDN kernels depends on which of the raced configurations wins, the race does not converge, and a boot's speed is set at compile time by a race the user cannot see and the cache then replays. The pin cell (cold7a's winners forced first on a normal cache) is the one that would put a number on the flag itself; it is registered and not run. The half-shared trajectory (16 of 32 with cold7a, against 0 with every cache-on boot) is noted and not read: it may be the two shared kernels, or the flag's in-process state, and nothing in hand separates those. sa0_15b follows.

## The slow side has a boot state, and the inventory names it (2026-09-05, 10:43Z by the shell clock)

Threadchip's message of this hour recalled a finding of mine from 2026-09-02 (private bench note, bisect #2: one compile-cache artefact, 749fe98e, made every boot that loaded it slow, and dropping it restored the rate to the decimal) and proposed that tonight's slow side is a partial recompile: a boot that loads some of its compiled artefacts and recompiles the rest. His own box then said partial boots are harmless there (ten of them inside its band on 0.28), so it came to this tree as a hypothesis to test, at no cost in boots. The lane0 inventory (regenerated with stat: the committed first body was BusyBox usage text, replaced) gives every artefact's write time, and each cell file gives its boot window (the first engine INFO timestamp to the file's modification time), so every card-0 boot can be classified by how many artefacts it wrote: three (a full compile), none (a full load), one or two (partial). analyze_lineage.py in the flightbench raw directory does that; its table, card 0 only, steady ms per step:

- PARTIAL-2 (loaded artefact 1, recompiled 2 and 3): std7 45.1, a7off2 44.9, cgn7b 46.5; also ff7b 45.0 and a15on2 84.9, each with its own flag as a confound.
- PARTIAL-1 (loaded 1 and 2, recompiled 3): blk7 23.4, ffg7 23.1.
- FULL compile at normal configuration: a7off 23.2, t8 23.2, sa0_15 23.6, t11 23.4, sa0_7a 23.6 (ff7a 46.9 and ff15 49.5 under the forced first configuration; cgn7a 33.7 with graphs off).
- FULL load at normal configuration: t7b, t7c, t7d 23.2, r7a to r7d 23.2 to 23.3 (q16_7 63.5 at the query maximum of 16; lk15off 56.6 at width 15).

Every slow boot this record had left unexplained is a partial-2 boot; no partial-2 boot is fast; no partial-1, full-compile or full-load boot at a normal configuration is slow. The default lineage the fast loaders use is itself assembled from three boots (artefact 1 from w7 at 04:39Z, artefact 2 from std7's recompile at 07:20Z, artefact 3 from blk7's at 07:29Z), and r7b to r7d loaded std7's recompiled artefact and were fast, so on 0.28 on this card it is the partial boot itself that runs slow, not the later loaders of what it wrote; that is the opposite of the 2026-09-02 reading (0.27.1, loaders slow), and both may be true of their versions. Why a boot ends up partial is not read here: the key of artefacts 2 and 3 evidently differs between boots whose artefact 1 matches, and what enters that key is the maintainer's question. The pair that decides it, registered before it runs, on card 0 with card 1 running pc0c (neighbour recorded; the effect under test is 2x): r7e is a default boot with the lineage's artefacts 2 and 3 (75da7859, 04cf860e) moved to a held directory on the volume, so it must load artefact 1 and recompile two; predicted 45; a 23 falsifies partial-2 as sufficient. r7f is a default boot after it, which loads all three; predicted 23; a 45 says the slot r7e wrote poisons its loaders, the 2026-09-02 reading. Both captured by the late watcher (the names match its pattern).

Addendum (10:44Z by the shell clock, before r7e lands). Threadchip extracted the load/compile sequence from sixty native logs: three partial-2 boots at normal configuration on the 3090 (d7na 25.2, ffc2 25.5 under the forced first configuration, nx2 25.1) all sit inside that card's 24.7 to 25.5 band, beside partial-1 boots at 25.0 to 25.2. So the class exists on both cards and is slow only here: partial-2 is not sufficient on its own; it needs this card, this host, or a condition not isolated. r7e stays the test of sufficiency on this card. Registered now, conditional on r7e landing at 45: r7g, a default boot with only artefact 2 held aside (load 1 and 3, recompile 2), which says which module's recompile carries the cost and turns "a partial boot is slow" into "recompiling module N while loading the others is slow", the form a maintainer can act on; if r7g is fast, artefact 3 is tried the same way. Provenance labels kept: bisect #2 and 749fe98e are this tree's (2026-09-02 bench note); the "module 2's key depends on whether module 1 was loaded or compiled" sentence is threadchip's recollection of a refinement, unverified here until re-found.

Addendum (10:46Z by the shell clock; r7e still booting). The three artefacts have names in the engine log, and this tree's captures print them: "Using cache directory: .../rank_0_0/backbone", ".../dflash_head", ".../dflash2_candidate_selector" (the late captures of cgn7a, t11, cgn7b). Read in log order, cgn7b (slow) is L:6cfcec20 then D:dflash_head C:8.68 s then D:dflash2_candidate_selector C:0.45 s, so it loaded the backbone and compiled the head and the selector; t11 (fast) compiled all three (backbone 55.40 s, head 13.02 s, selector 0.98 s); r7b (fast) loaded all three. With names, the two partial classes read: partial-1 = backbone loaded, head loaded, selector recompiled (blk7 23.4, ffg7 23.1, fast); partial-2 = backbone loaded, head compiled, selector recompiled (std7, a7off2, cgn7b, 45 to 46.5, slow). The selector is recompiled in both, so it is controlled, and the one difference between the fast partials and the slow partials is whether the DFlash2 head's graph was loaded or compiled while the backbone was loaded. Every full compile at a normal configuration (backbone and head both compiled) is fast, so it is not compiling the head that costs; it is compiling the head against a loaded backbone, on this card. On the 3090 the same class (backbone loaded, head compiled: d7na, ffc2, nx2) runs in band. r7g (hold only artefact 2) is therefore dropped as redundant before it was written: holding only artefact 3 is what the partial-1 boots already are, and holding only artefact 2 is the slow class by construction. What r7e adds is sufficiency on this card (a boot forced into the class on purpose), r7f whether the head it writes poisons its loaders. The line for the maintainer, if r7e confirms: recompiling the DFlash2 head's torch.compile graph while the backbone's is loaded from the cache runs the whole step about 2x slow on a 4090 under WSL2 and is free on a 3090; the head is fork code (the drafter's own graph), and the mixed state arises whenever the head's cache key changes and the backbone's does not, which the record shows happening between boots whose engine configuration lines match. (Threadchip supplied the names from his log; verified here before use.)

This tree's captures show the same split: head compiled after a compiled backbone 13.62 (cgn7a), 13.35 (sa0_7a), 13.02 (t11) s; after a loaded backbone 8.68 (cgn7b), 8.94 (r7e) s; with the cache disabled 10.89 and 10.70 s (cold7a, cold7b, no cache-directory lines). What the short compile skips is not in this tree's captures either (TRITON_PRINT_AUTOTUNING does not see inductor's own benchmarking); the inductor cache files carry a kernel_perf and a best_config that a maintainer can diff between the two classes. ## r7e: the slow side reproduced on purpose (2026-09-05, 10:54Z by the shell clock)

r7e (the launcher default with the lineage's head and selector artefacts held aside on lane0; RESOLVED compiled=1 aot_loaded=2; sequence backbone loaded, head compiled in 8.94 s, selector loaded from an older matching artefact): 45.1 ms per step steady (44.7 to 46.3), tokens per step 3.708, round 7.000, 81.9 tok/s, four completions under 1024. Three boots of the class by accident (std7, a7off2, cgn7b) and one on purpose, four of four slow; nine full loads and five full compiles at normal configurations, none slow; two boots that loaded the head, none slow. The trajectory is its own (2 of 32 rows identical with the default, 2 with std7), where std7 had replayed the default to the token, so the class does not fix the numerics one way; what it fixes is the step cost. The note to Mads gets item 11 with this number; r7f (up 10:54Z, the loader of what r7e wrote) says whether the slot poisons loaders, predicted no.

Addendum (10:55Z by the shell clock). With std7 (32 of 32 identical to the default at 45 against 23) the claim this record supports is the narrow one: the slow state can occur with the default trajectory exactly, so speed and numerics are separable; r7e's own trajectory (2 of 32) is the ordinary compile lottery measured all night and says nothing more. Note item 11 and the write-up now say it that way. His side is committed in his private gates (class census, module names, compile-time split, lcl7, text identity, the restore defect and its verification boot).

Addendum (10:56Z by the shell clock; r7f at its first rows). r7f's sequence is backbone loaded (2bc92aab), head loaded (75da7859, the artefact r7e wrote), selector compiled (0.82 s): it did not load the selector artefact 9796c0cd that r7e had loaded. So the selector's cache key depends on whether the head was loaded or compiled in the same boot (r7e compiled the head and matched an old selector artefact; r7f loaded the head and had to recompile), which is the refinement threadchip recalled from my 2026-09-02 work ("a module's key depends on whether the previous module was loaded or compiled"), now observed in this tree for the head-selector pair and offered as the reason boots at matching configurations keep landing partial: the keys chain, so a boot that loads one graph can be forced to recompile the next. r7f is therefore the partial-1 class (head loaded, selector recompiled), predicted fast like blk7 and ffg7; a 23 also answers the poisoned-loader question in the negative, since it loads r7e's head.

Addendum (10:57Z by the shell clock). The chained key reproduces on the 3090 independently (his lcl7 loaded a different selector artefact from his full-load boots with nothing but the head's load-or-compile state changed), and it has a consequence visible without an experiment: the cache holds one artefact per chain state, not one per module, so downstream modules accumulate fastest. His native cache: 70 artefacts, 6.8 GB, 16 backbone-class, 21 head-class, 33 selector-class. This box, counted by size class (backbone above 100 MB, head 10 to 100 MB, selector under 10 MB): lane0 60 artefacts, 6.7 GB, 15 backbone, 21 head, 24 selector; lane1 41 artefacts, 3.2 GB, 8 backbone, 14 head, 19 selector. Three modules, and on every volume the count rises down the chain. This is upstream vLLM rather than the fork, checkable by anyone with one directory listing, and it goes into the note as item 12, separate from the slow-boot finding, which needs the 4090; the two share a cause (the chained keys are why matched configurations keep landing partial) and not a consequence.

## Correction: no "cache off, align kept" boot exists in this arc (2026-09-05, 11:11Z by the shell clock)

The image's model configuration code overrides the mamba cache mode whenever prefix caching is disabled: vllm/model_executor/models/config.py, class MambaModelConfig.verify_and_update_config, lines 600 to 633 of the installed file, the else branch of "if cache_config.enable_prefix_caching": if the mode is not "none" it is set to "none" with the warning "Mamba cache mode is set to 'none' when prefix caching is disabled" (and in the if branch a mode of "none" becomes "align" by default, with an info line). Read live from p7a's engine log while it booted: the non-default args line reports mamba_cache_mode: 'align' with enable_prefix_caching: False (the arguments as passed by the launcher plus the cell's --no-enable-prefix-caching), and two seconds later "WARNING [config.py:633] Mamba cache mode is set to 'none' when prefix caching is disabled". So the non-default args line reports the argument, not the resolved mode, for this setting; the resolved mode is in the warning.

Consequences, each a correction to something this record or the write-up said. First, every cache-off boot in this arc, on both boxes and at both widths, ran with the mamba cache mode none, whatever flag was passed: pc0a to pc0d, p7a and p7b here, and the native arms described as "cache off, align kept" (the width-7 pair and m15a, m15b), which need their logs read for the same warning before anything else is said about them. Second, "prefix caching alone moved card 1 at width 15 with align kept (29 to 17 of 32)" is withdrawn: the cache-off pair was also mode none. Third, the native result "cache off with align kept replays 8 of 8; align off differs 8 of 8" cannot mean what it said, because both arms resolve to none if the warning fired in both; what differed between those arms is then unknown (the compile lineage of the r7e and r7f kind is one candidate; the cache-off boots here diverge with identical loaded artefacts, so not that alone). Fourth, item 10 of the note (align is the determinism flag; the launcher sets it only as a side effect; move it outside the conditional) is wrong in its mechanism and its advice: moving the flag would do nothing, because the engine overrides it, and the finding that stands is narrower and different: prefix caching off means mamba cache mode none, always, silently except for one warning line, and the engine's arguments line reports the passed value. Item 10 is rewritten to that. Fifth, the registered question for p7a and p7b changes: they are width 7, cache off, mode none, card 1, a two-boot replay count at that condition, not a card-versus-width test with align held. What does stand: the launcher default (cache on, mode align) replays across boots on both boxes; cache-off boots (mode none) diverge here with identical artefacts; what the divergence is made of is open. The rule this adds to lesson 74's "read the engine line": a setting the engine can override is read from the override's own log line, and a resolved-arguments line is not the resolved configuration for it.

Addendum (11:13Z by the shell clock). Threadchip read his logs: the warning fires in exactly his "align kept" arms (d7ma, d7mb, m15a, m15b, all resolved none), his "align off" arms passed nothing and were none already (cache.py:141 defaults to none), and only his cache-on boots ran align. Both of his arm types resolved to the same mode; "align is the determinism flag" is retracted on his side in full and pulled from item 10, the write-up and this record's conclusions (the earlier addenda that carried it stay as the record of how it was believed). What his rows show under the one resolved configuration, prefix caching off and mode none: two pairs identical on 8 of 8 (d7ma with d7mb, m15a with m15b) and two observations split (d7na against d7nb 0 of 8; nx1 to nx3). Identical configuration, mixed outcomes: boot-level nondeterminism in the cache-off condition, about half the pairs matching there, beside this box's card 1 (pc0a with pc0c identical, pc0b and pc0d each their own). With caching on, every boot tested on either box replayed. That is a description at low power, not a mechanism, and it goes into the write-up as exactly that: cache-off boots are sometimes reproducible and sometimes not. The lesson he names is lesson 90's: a guard that reads a passed value is not a guard.

## sa0_15b: the kernel-off replicate at width 15, timing held and the one-token row reproduced (2026-09-05, 11:34Z by the shell clock)

sa0_15b (width 15, SPEC_ATTN=0, card 0, card 1 idle; boot sequence backbone loaded from sa0_15's artefact, head compiled in 8.55 s, selector compiled, so a member of the slow class; RESOLVED num_speculative_tokens 15): 23.6 ms per step steady (23.4 to 24.3) against sa0_15's 23.6 and the kernel-on 56.5, tokens per step 3.732 against sa0_15's 3.720 and std1's 3.739, round 7.185, 155.9 tok/s, per-position acceptance within two per thousand of the kernel-on boots, 18 of 32 rows identical with sa0_15 (a different compile lineage) and 0 of 32 with std1. The timing replicates, the acceptance is equal within a boot's spread, and the kernel is not what costs at width 15 when it is off even in a boot of the slow class. The completion lengths, every one read: 31 rows of 32 run to a natural end or to 1024 (the short ones are prompt 3 seed 1 at 242 tokens and prompt 7's four seeds at 378 to 834, the same prompts that stop short in every boot), and one row is a one-token completion at prompt 5 seed 2, the same prompt and seed as sa0_15's, two boots of two. The same prompt and seed (and seed 3) return one token under the forced first Triton configuration at width 7 (ff7a, ff7b, two of two), run to 1024 in every kernel-on boot at either width, and run to a full answer at greedy under the forced configuration (ffg7 here, and natively). Classification, at the second review's standard: the kernel-off path is a suspect for the output only in the sense that any change of numerics is; prompt 5's first-token distribution puts an end-of-sequence token near the top, and two unrelated kernel switches move it onto seed 2's sample, reproducibly across boots of each switch, while the argmax stays a full answer. So SPEC_ATTN=0 is a workaround that is not output-neutral: it changes sampled text the way a kernel change does and turns one boundary seed of this cohort into an empty completion; a user who takes the 2.4x should know that, and a maintainer who fixes the kernel's cost at a query maximum of 16 removes the reason to take it. The card-0 queue is empty.

Addendum (11:37Z by the shell clock). Native readings registered before they land, from the other box: sasd0 and sasd1 (width 7, seeds 1 to 4, kernel off against on) test sa0_7a's observation that the switch at width 7 leaves prompt 5 seed 2 intact; sa15sd0 and sa15sd1 (width 15, the same seeds) are the matched cell for the one-token row. If seed 2 empties there at width 15 with the kernel off, the boundary seed is a property of the stock verify path at that width and universal, and the note's caveat belongs beside the recommendation for everyone; if it does not, the caveat is scoped to this card and the note says so, because a user elsewhere would otherwise decline a 2.4x fix over a warning that does not apply. Threadchip's own naming of the pattern behind three mismatched cells today, kept for the journal: each time the arm varied the interesting variable and let the width or the flag drift, checking that the arm varies what is intended and not that it holds everything else where the other box holds it.

Addendum (11:49Z by the shell clock). Native width-7 sweep in: sasd0 (kernel off) and sasd1 (kernel on), seeds 1 to 4, 32 rows each, no short rows, prompt 5 at 1024 on every seed both ways. So at width 7 the kernel switch alone leaves the boundary seed intact on both cards, and sa0_7a's one-boot observation has a four-seed second card behind it. The empties seen across the arc are two triggers on one seed: the forced first Triton configuration at width 7 (both cards) and the stock verify path at width 15 (this card, two boots); what they share is the seed's distance from a boundary, not a flag. The matched width-15 pair (sa15sd0, sa15sd1) is running there and decides whether the width-15 caveat is a property of the stock verify path or of this card.

# Arc three: drafters (2026-09-05, 13:09Z by the shell clock)

Binding question, Michael's: do the two community drafters that exist for this exact target beat the DFlash2 head we run, on the same cohort, at matched settings; and is the block-16 drafter's trained tail worth more than the lookup's. The first of the three free things (narrower blocks) is already answered by arc two's width sweep (widths 3 to 7, one boot each, card 0: accepted tokens fall monotonically with the width at the same step cost, so 7 stays), and the third (the hidden-state hypothesis) needs a fine-tune and comes after these. Drafters: onewhosighs/Apathy-Qwen3.8-27B-DFlash-drafter-v2 (plain DFlash, DFlashDraftModel, 6 layers, block_size 16, BF16 3.96 GiB, target layers 1, 10, 18, 27, 35, 44, 52, 61, mask token 248077, trained against unsloth/Qwen3.8-27B-NVFP4 per its card, last modified 2026-08-24) and RadixArk/Qwen3.8-27B-DSpark (DSparkDraftModel, 5 layers, BF16 3.5 GiB, target layers 5, 19, 33, 47, 61, serving block 7, training block 16, card mean acceptance 3.43 on NVFP4 and FP8 targets, trained with SpecForge). Both downloaded to the models directory this box mounts at /app/models (huggingface_hub snapshot, safetensors and configs). Harness: arc two's cell script derived unchanged except for the drafter (W4A16 target, CTX=fast, the eight-prompt cohort at the model's own sampling, seeds 1 to 4, 32 rows, one boot per cell, card 0 with card 1 idle, the perpos client, the RESOLVED line now carrying the boot's load-or-compile sequence). Serving path: the image launcher honours DRAFT from the environment and its SPEC_CFG method is already "dflash" (line 219), so the Apathy drafter runs through SPEC=dflash2 with LOOKUP=0 (the fork's lookup lives in the dflash2 model class); DSpark needs one sed on that line (method dflash to dspark) and its first boot omits the draft_sample_method field. Cells and predictions, registered before any boot: ap7 (K=7, QMAX 8; predicted below DFlash2's 3.798 tokens per step, the card says acceptance dips near position 5); ap11 (K=11, QMAX 12, fast on this card, async on with the KV pin raised as in a15on2); ap15sa0 (K=15 with the verify kernel off, timing representative on this card and not output-neutral; the kernel-on K=15 belongs to the 3090, asked of threadchip with the matched-cell rule); ds7 (K=7; predicted at or below DFlash2). Baselines on the same cohort: DFlash2 K=7 23.2 ms per step and 3.798 tokens per step (nine boots), K=15 synchronous 56.5 and 3.739, K=15 kernel off 23.6 and 3.732. Readouts: tokens per step (accepted plus bonus), ms per step, tok/s, per-position acceptance, completion lengths, the sequence line. A cell with NO HEALTH is void and its FAILLOG is the reading (a plain DFlash checkpoint has not been served by this fork before).

Addendum (13:10Z by the shell clock). Threadchip's registration (his private gates file) before any boot caught a mismatch on his side and corrected a quote on mine: his DFlash2 baselines were 8-row boots (one seed per prompt; K=7 25.0 ms per step and 4.019 tokens per step over d7a, d7b, vrf7 and lcl7; K=15 25.5 and 4.068), so he is adding bl7 and bl15 at 32 rows and seeds 1 to 4 as the matched baselines; my quote of his K=7 figure as "about 3.9" was from memory and is struck (4.019 is the number). This box's baselines are 32-row boots already (r7a to r7d, t7b to t7d, the same client), so 23.2 and 3.798 stand as matched. His three risks, adopted here: the width-15 stride assert from issue 73 if K=15 with LOOKUP=0 trips it (read the log, do not work around it); both community drafters were trained against quantized targets (Apathy on NVFP4, DSpark on NVFP4 and FP8) while we serve W4A16, so a hidden-state mismatch would depress acceptance for reasons unrelated to drafter quality, a caveat that sits beside every number of this arc; and the width-15 KV edge (retry, do not lower max_model_len). He abstains on the ap15 prediction, as this record does.

Addendum (13:12Z by the shell clock, before ap7 lands). Two things from the other box's pre-boot checks, adopted. First, the comparison at K=7 is not like-for-like: Apathy's block_size is 16 and its card says trained_drafts = block_size - 1, so at K=7 it serves at half its trained width against a DFlash2 head serving at its own width (block 8, K=7), and any deficit at ap7 may be mostly that; ap15 is the matched drafter comparison, with the note that DFlash2 at K=15 is its 7-token head plus the lookup for positions 7 to 14, so ap15 compares a trained tail with a lookup tail, which is the question Michael asked. Second, the mask token id: Apathy's config carries 248077 inside dflash_config (None at the top level; DSpark 248070 at both levels; our DFlash2 248070 inside dflash_config), and threadchip's read of the model code says the only consumer is the separate-mask-embedding path, gated on a mask tensor neither checkpoint ships, so it is not a confound. His bl7 (DFlash2, 32 rows, seeds 1 to 4) is running natively; bl15, ap7, ap15, ds7 follow there.

Addendum (13:13Z by the shell clock). Reading the nested level properly on both sides settles how the arc is read, and it withdraws "ap15 is the matched comparison" (his and half of mine). dflash_config.block_size is 16 for Apathy (trained drafts 15) and 8 for our DFlash2 (trained drafts 7; the earlier "no constraint" reading looked only at the top level); DSpark is 7 at the top level. So at K=7 DFlash2 serves at its trained width and Apathy at half of its; at K=15 Apathy serves at its trained width and DFlash2 at double its own, the extra positions coming from the lookup. Neither K compares the two drafters at a width both were built for, and no such configuration exists without a third drafter. The useful reading: ap7 measures Apathy handicapped and says little about the drafter; ap15 measures DFlash2's lookup tail against Apathy's trained tail, which is not a drafter comparison but arc two's question asked from the other side, and the one Michael asked. And the cheapest confirmation of the arc, sitting in a nested key the whole time: the seam arc two found empirically at position 7, with positions 7 to 14 earning 1 to 2 percent, is exactly block_size minus 1 for a block-8 head; the field predicts the seam.

ap7sa0 boots (2026-09-05, 13:20Z by the shell clock). The same cell with SPEC_ATTN=0 loaded the same way (18.09 GiB, backbone compiled 55.73 s, head 7.91 s, a new cache key), captured graphs and reached "Application startup complete"; the client is running its rows. So the illegal memory access of ap7 needs the fork's split-KV verify attention in the path, or the memory the kernel's buffers take: ap7lite (kernel on, MAX_LEN=16384, KV_MEM empty) is the next boot and separates those two; if it dies the same way with the memory freed, the kernel is incompatible with the plain DFlash class in this fork, which is a finding for the maintainer beside items 9 and 11 (a plain DFlash drafter is a supported vLLM path that the fork's patch breaks), and every Apathy number in this arc then comes from the kernel-off path with the output caveat that path carries at width 15.

Native baselines (2026-09-05, 13:22Z by the shell clock; reported from threadchip's private logs). bl7 (DFlash2, K=7, LOOKUP=0, 32 rows, seeds 1 to 4): 25.4 ms per step, tokens per step median 3.892, mean 3.905. bl7on (the same with LOOKUP=1, the launcher default): 25.5, median 3.888, mean 3.765. Paired by prompt and seed: mean difference -0.139 tokens per step (sd 0.324, se 0.057, t -2.43 at 31 degrees of freedom), median difference -0.015, 18 of 32 rows worse with lookup drafting on, the largest losses on the rows that draft best without it (prompt 5 seeds 3 and 4 from 5.3 and 5.2 down to 4.1 and 4.5; prompt 2 seed 1 from 2.17 to 1.64), the largest gains 0.35 or less. So on this cohort lookup drafting at K=7 costs about 3.6 percent of accepted tokens per step at unchanged step cost, significant at the row level, with his caveats kept: one boot per arm (the 32 cells are not 32 boots, the interval is a floor), one cohort of eight prompts that neither quotes nor reproduces its context, and a quote-heavy or code-editing cohort could plausibly reverse the sign. This is the mechanism arc two never measured at width 7 (the extension cannot engage there; the drafting substitution can), and it settles the baseline for the arc: the community drafters are compared against bl7 (lookup off), with the lookup delta named between bl7 and the shipped configuration. Locally, bl7off (queued behind the diagnostics) against the nine identical default boots at 3.798, paired by prompt and seed, is the same measurement on this box. If it replicates here, it is a candidate item 13 for the note: lookup drafting is a small net loss on prose that does not reproduce its context, at the width users run.

ap7sa0 first rows (2026-09-05, 13:23Z by the shell clock): 153.2 and 145.6 ms per step on prompts 0 and 1 (25.2 and 16.1 tok/s; tokens per step 3.860 and 2.346), against 23 ms per step for the DFlash2 head on the same rows. That is not a drafter property: the boot's capture line reads "Graph capturing finished in 6 secs, took 0.00 GiB" with only FULL captures (8 of 8) and no PIECEWISE capture, where a default boot captures 5 FULL and 5 PIECEWISE and takes 1.36 GiB, so on the plain DFlash path in this stack the compiled backbone runs without its piecewise graphs, launch-bound at about six times the step cost. The Apathy cells therefore read on acceptance only (tokens per step and the per-position curve, paired by prompt and seed against bl7off and the default), and their speed is a property of the fork or of vLLM's dflash path on this hybrid model, not of the drafter; the same caution applies to ds7 and to threadchip's Apathy boots. The cell runs to its 32 rows for the acceptance columns (about 20 minutes at this step cost).

Native ap7 (2026-09-05, 13:27Z by the shell clock; reported). Threadchip's first Apathy boot on the 3090 died at KV allocation, before graph capture, with torch.OutOfMemoryError (tried to allocate 5.20 GiB with 4.95 GiB free): the launcher pins the KV cache at 5.2 GiB regardless of drafter size, and a 4.0 GiB bf16 drafter in place of the 1.2 GiB W4A16 head leaves too little on a 24 GiB card there (this box had 22.45 GiB free at the same point and reserved the pin). His reading rule, adopted: a boot that dies earlier for an unrelated reason is a could-not-run and must never be read as clean, so "the IMA does not reproduce on the 3090" is not a sentence this record contains; his ap7b with KV_MEM=4.0e9 is the honest test and reports the pin as resolved, the capture lines by type, and whether an IMA or another OOM appeared. Two things stand independent of the retry: the launcher's KV pin does not account for drafter size (a usability finding for the note beside the community-drafter results: a bf16 drafter on a 24 GiB card gets a hard OOM at boot with no hint that the pin is the cause), and his bl7 capture line (6 FULL and 11 PIECEWISE, 1.33 GiB) matches this box's default boots, so the FULL-only, 0.00 GiB capture on the plain DFlash path here stands out against a matched baseline on both cards.

Native ap7b and ap7c (2026-09-05, 13:29Z by the shell clock; reported). ap7b with KV_MEM=4.0e9 honoured died at the second wall: ValueError, a context of 65536 needs 5.18 GiB with 3.7 GiB available. ap7c (MAX_LEN=16384, no pin) is running, matched to this box's ap7lite. Neither failure reached graph capture, so the illegal memory access remains untested from that card. The usability finding is two-sided: a bf16 community drafter on a 24 GiB card fails at the launcher's defaults, and the obvious fix fails too, because lowering the pin leaves max_model_len at 65536 and the engine refuses again with a different error class whose advice repeats the first one; the working combination is a shorter context plus no pin, which neither message suggests. The launcher's memory defaults assume a quantized drafter of about 1.2 GiB; both community drafters (4.0 and 3.5 GiB bf16) break them on a 24 GiB card. His caveat on ap7c, matched here for ap7lite: at a 16384 context no step cost is comparable to the 65536 baselines, so those cells read on acceptance only, paired by prompt and seed, which is also the only reading the missing piecewise captures allow; two independent reasons, same conclusion.

Correction before it cost a cell (2026-09-05, 13:31Z by the shell clock). The launcher honours MAX_LEN from the environment at line 150 and then, on the DFlash path, overwrites it unconditionally at line 360 with DFLASH_MAX_LEN (default 65536); threadchip found it when his ap7c resolved max_model_len 65536 and died at the same wall, and my ap7lite carried the same defect (MAX_LEN=16384, which would have resolved to 65536 and hit the second wall instead of testing the kernel). Fixed here before ap7lite booted: the chain was stopped mid-cell (ap7sa0's container detached and left to finish its rows; its summary lines will not appear in drafters.out, the cell file is complete on its own), ap7lite re-armed with DFLASH_MAX_LEN=16384 and KV_MEM empty, and the RESOLVED line now prints the resolved max_model_len and kv_cache_memory_bytes first, before any other status, so a silent override cannot pass as a configured arm. This is the fourth passed-versus-resolved trap of the day (the cache mode overridden by the engine, LOOKUP with no engine echo, the arguments line as an echo, and now a launcher variable overwritten by the launcher two hundred lines after it is read), and the usability line for the note hardens with it: a user swapping in a bf16 drafter on a 24 GiB card must change both the KV pin and the context, the context variable on the DFlash path is not the documented one, and none of the three error messages says so.

## ap7sa0: the block-16 drafter at width 7, kernel off, 16 rows (2026-09-05, 13:33Z by the shell clock)

The cell ended at 16 rows (seeds 1 and 2 of all eight prompts), not 32: stopping the chain script mid-cell to fix ap7lite broke the docker attach that carried the client's output, and the container exited two minutes later at 13:32:58Z. My doing; lesson 95. The 16 rows are a valid half-cell, paired on the same 16 rows of the baseline. Apathy (block 16, plain DFlash) at K=7 with the fork's verify kernel off, LOOKUP=0: tokens per step 3.205 against the default DFlash2 boot's 3.798 on the same rows, paired mean -0.620 (se 0.147, t -4.22, median -0.637, worse on 14 of 16), round 7.000; per-position acceptance per thousand rounds 212, 139, 92, 63, 45, 33, 24 against 201, 146, 110, 85, 67, 54, 43: a higher first position and a steeper fall after it. Three completions under 1024 (prompt 4 seed 1 at 143, prompt 7 seeds 1 and 2), no one-token row. Step cost 146 ms (144 to 154), 21.8 tok/s, not a drafter property (the plain DFlash path captured no piecewise graphs here; the resolved line and the capture line are in the file). The registered prediction (ap7 below DFlash2) holds, with the registered caveat that Apathy serves at half its trained width here and was trained against an NVFP4 target; the like-for-like baseline (bl7off, lookup off) is queued and the delta against it will be smaller by the lookup's cost at 7 if the native 3.6 percent replicates.

Native ap7d (2026-09-05, 13:35Z by the shell clock; reported). DFLASH_MAX_LEN=16384 resolved correctly and the boot still died: 16384 tokens need 1.98 GiB of KV and 0.8 GiB was available, because with the pin empty the pool is what GPU_UTIL 0.93 leaves after the target, a 4.0 GiB bf16 drafter, and the V2 runner's 1.9 GiB graph reservation (VLLM_V2_CUDAGRAPH_MEM_MIB). So the memory finding is not that the pin is wrong: three budgets are set independently (the KV pin at 5.2 GiB, GPU_UTIL at 0.93, the graph reservation at 1900 MiB) and none knows the drafter's size; a 1.2 GiB W4A16 drafter fits in the slack and a 4.0 GiB bf16 one does not, and every combination of two of the three fails on a 24 GiB card. That is the shape for the note, and it applies to DSpark at 3.5 GiB. His ap7e (context 4096, kernel on) is running with ap7f (the same with SPEC_ATTN=0) chained as the fallback so the acceptance data is not held hostage to the kernel question. His reading of ap7sa0's curve, adopted as the hypothesis for ap15: a higher first position (212 against 201) and a steeper tail (24 against 43 by position 6) is what a drafter trained for a 16-block would look like when asked for 7, front-loaded; if the handicap is real the curve should flatten at K=15. This box's ap7lite has the same three budgets and 22.45 GiB free at the same point, so it may reach the kernel where his card could not; its RESOLVED line decides which reading applies.

Native ap7f and the floor (2026-09-05, 13:44Z by the shell clock; reported). ap7f (context 4096, SPEC_ATTN=0, this box's working configuration) died at the same wall as the kernel-on arms: 0.8 GiB available for the KV cache, and even 4096 tokens need more, because on this hybrid model the mamba page makes the per-request state, not the token count, the floor. So the kernel is not involved in any of the six native failures, his wall and this box's illegal memory access are unrelated problems (a memory floor there, a kernel fault with 22 GiB to spare here), and the only knob with room is GPU_UTIL, which none of the error messages names (they point at max_model_len). ap7g runs at GPU_UTIL=0.97 with an 8192 context, kernel on, on an idle card, a setting he would not recommend as a default. The note's sentence sharpens to: on a 24 GiB card this stack cannot serve a 4 GiB bf16 drafter at the shipped gpu_memory_utilization at any context length, and the error never says so; DSpark at 3.5 GiB will very likely meet the same floor there, so his ds7 starts from GPU_UTIL. If ap7g does not serve, the 3090's contribution to "see how they perform" is that it cannot run either community drafter without departing from the shipped memory configuration, which is a result, not a number.

ap7lite could-not-run (2026-09-05, 13:45Z by the shell clock). Resolved max_model_len 16384 (the corrected variable held), loaded 18.09 GiB, compiled both graphs, and died before capture with the ValueError that a 16384 context needs more KV than is available: with the pin empty, GPU_UTIL 0.90 minus the target, the 4 GiB drafter and the graph reservation leaves the same floor threadchip hit at 0.93. Never reached the kernel; not evidence either way about the illegal memory access. The kernel-on test on this box therefore uses his ap7g design, matched: GPU_UTIL=0.97 (card 1 idle), DFLASH_MAX_LEN=8192, KV_MEM empty, SPEC_ATTN default. The kernel-off width cells do not need it: ap7sa0 served with the pin and the 65536 context, so ap11sa0 and ap15sa0 run with the default memory (the pin raised by 256 MiB for async at K above 7, as in a15on2) and the kernel off, and a kernel-off DSpark follows ds7 in case ds7 dies the same way. All queued in a second chain behind the running one; the running chain is not touched (lesson 95).

Native ap7g serves (2026-09-05, 13:51Z by the shell clock; reported). GPU_UTIL=0.97 with an 8192 context: 1.74 GiB available for the KV cache (0.8 at 0.93), 10 FULL and 12 PIECEWISE graphs captured, the fork's verify kernel on, past capture and serving rows. Two readings change. First, the illegal memory access does not reproduce on the 3090 at a boot that passed the stage where it happens here, with the kernel in the path: one boot on one card, so the supported reading is that this box's fault is card- or host-specific (or tied to the memory state of that boot), and "the kernel breaks the plain DFlash path" is withdrawn as a general claim; note item 14 is softened to what stands. Second, the FULL-only capture that "took 0.00 GiB" on ap7sa0 is not a property of the plain DFlash path, because his boot captured both kinds normally; the candidate is memory: a capture that degrades silently when memory is short would explain the missing piecewise graphs, the zero bytes and the 146 ms per step in one mechanism, and it may also be the state under this box's illegal access. This box's matched ap7g (0.97, 8192, kernel on) is the test: piecewise captures appearing there mean the earlier boot was starved, not structural, and the launch-bound reading was a memory symptom. His caveat, adopted for both boxes: 0.97 and 8192 differ from the baselines (0.93 or 0.90, 65536), so those cells quote acceptance paired by prompt and seed and no step cost; a baseline re-run at the same setting is one more boot, worth it only if the acceptance result makes speed interesting.

ds7 void (2026-09-05, 13:56Z by the shell clock). The DSpark cell resolved method dspark and the DSparkDraftModel class at the default pin and context and died at model load, before any kernel: "ValueError: hf_overrides must be a dict for get_quant_config to get the quantization config from it", raised from vllm/model_executor/model_loader/weight_utils.py:293 via config/vllm.py get_quantization_config, called by model_executor/models/utils.py get_draft_quant_config from v1/worker/gpu/spec_decode/dspark/utils.py:48 (load_dspark_model), which overrides the draft's quant config because the post-init restores the target's. The drafter is bf16 with no quantization_config, so the lookup should return None; instead the draft config's inherited quantization sends it through the target's hf_overrides, which SpeculativeConfig composes as a callable for the DFlash-family drafters, and the loader refuses a callable. A vLLM 0.28 dspark-path defect on a quantized target with composed overrides, not a fork patch (the fork's launcher passes no overrides); SGLang's recipe for this drafter passes an explicit unquantized draft, which vLLM's config has no field for. Retry registered: ds7q, the same cell with a one-line in-container patch making get_draft_quant_config return None when the draft config carries no quantization_config; void again if it dies elsewhere, and the FAILLOG is the reading.

Native matched K=7 (2026-09-05, 14:02Z by the shell clock; reported). ap7g (Apathy) against bl7g (DFlash2), both at GPU_UTIL 0.97, an 8192 context, LOOKUP=0, the fork's verify kernel on, 32 rows, one boot per arm, both boots capturing normally (10 FULL and 12 PIECEWISE): tokens per step 3.000 against 3.832 (paired -0.832, -21.7 percent, se 0.252, t -3.30, worse on 25 of 32), step cost 30.9 against 25.6 ms (+20.7 percent), throughput 97.1 against 149.7 tok/s: at width 7 on that cohort the block-16 drafter delivers about 65 percent of DFlash2's throughput, worse on both axes. The configuration change itself is priced and small (bl7g against bl7: -0.072 tokens per step, -1.8 percent), so it does not explain the gap; his earlier unmatched -0.905 becomes -0.832 matched and is the number of record there, beside this box's -0.581 against bl7off on 16 rows; all three agree in direction and size. The speed half is single-card: his plain-DFlash boot captured normally and ran at 30.9 ms per step, where this box's captured FULL only at 0.00 GiB and ran at 146; either his 30.9 is the path's true cost and this box's boot was starved, or the path is slow on this host and fine on his; this box's ap7g at 0.97 decides (piecewise captures and a number near 30 make the starved reading right). What this says to Michael and what it does not: at width 7 on this cohort Apathy is worse on both axes and lands at about two thirds of DFlash2's throughput, two cards agreeing on acceptance and one on speed; it does not say Apathy is a worse drafter, because it serves at half its trained width against DFlash2 at exactly its own and was trained against an NVFP4 target where we serve W4A16, two handicaps in the same direction and neither measured; ap15 remains the only configuration where the comparison could go the other way. The note's sentence is therefore: at width 7, DSpark against DFlash2 is like-for-like on width and on target mismatch, and Apathy is not. His ds7 is queued behind his K=15 chain; bl15g is running there.
