Erratum, units (14:47Z by the shell clock; threadchip's catch). "The trained tail beats the lookup tail by about nine times" compared two different quantities: Apathy's tail per thousand rounds (54) against the lookup tail as a share of accepted tokens (0.6 percent). Converted both ways from bl15g's raw counts (7851 rounds, 21949 accepted at positions 0 to 6, 131 at 7 to 14): per thousand rounds, Apathy 54 against DFlash2 16.7, 3.2 times; as a share of accepted tokens, Apathy 8.3 percent against 0.59 percent, 13.9 times. The share answers Michael's question (does the trained tail beat the lookup tail as a contribution) and it is about 14 times; the rate is smaller because Apathy's whole curve is lower, so its tail is a larger slice of a smaller pie; both are true and say different things, the share goes in the note with the rate in a parenthesis. And his front-loading explanation of the width-7 deficit is withdrawn by him on this box's curve (his prediction was that the curve would flatten at the trained width; it steepened at positions 3 to 6), so the half-width caveat does not excuse the width-7 result as it was being written, and the note stops implying that it might. The arc's answer to the question asked, complete on this box's rows and independent of the second card: the block-16 drafter's trained tail beats DFlash2's lookup tail by about 14 times as a share of accepted tokens, so a wide block is worth something, and it still loses the total, because positions 0 to 6 are where the tokens are and it is worse at every one of them.

Correction (2026-09-05, 14:48Z by the shell clock; reported). ap15b on the 3090 did not run (ValueError: 1.87 GiB of KV needed, 1.41 available; no captures, no rows), the seventh could-not-run there, and the record marks it dead, not pending; "the second card on the answer" is withdrawn. What the seven walls now explain: halving the context from 8192 to 4096 moved the KV needed from 2.23 to 1.87 GiB, so about 1.5 GiB is fixed per-request state (the mamba and gated-delta-net page of this hybrid model) and only about 0.36 GiB scaled with the tokens; every context reduction worked on the small term while every error message recommended exactly that. The mechanism behind item 13, now rather than another instance of it: on a hybrid model the per-request state is the floor and max_model_len barely touches it. His next lever matches the mechanism: MAX_SEQS=1 (honoured from the environment at launcher line 340, unlike MAX_LEN), since these cells issue one request at a time and the default provisions four sequences of that fixed state. If ap15c fails too, the reportable result is that the block-16 drafter cannot run at its trained width on a 24 GiB card at all, a deployability answer for a drafter published for this exact target; bl15b (DFlash2 at 15 and 4096 on that card) is running to show whether the 1.2 GiB drafter fits where the 4.0 GiB one does not, which makes the point concrete. This box's 11 rows carry the acceptance answer on their own.

Native width-15 against width-7 for DFlash2, matched (2026-09-05, 14:53Z by the shell clock; reported). On the 3090 at an 8192 context, 32 rows each, one boot per arm: K=15 with the lookup on 3.973 tokens per step, 26.2 ms per step, 151.6 tok/s; K=7 3.832, 25.6, 149.5; the difference is 1.4 percent of throughput (at a 4096 context K=15 reads 141.3 tok/s, quoted only for the context cost, not mixed into the comparison). So reproduction mode is worth about one percent on hardware that does not pay the query-maximum-16 kernel cost, and is a loss on this 4090 host, where it pays 56.5 against 23.2 ms per step; the lookup tail at 0.59 percent of accepted tokens cannot produce more than a percent or two of throughput, and it does not. That is the cleanest answer to the question Michael opened with (whether 3.3 of 7 leaves throughput on the table): arc two established why the tail earns so little (the seam at block_size minus one; the head covers 7), and this prices the whole width-15 mode end to end at about one percent where it is free and strongly negative where it is not; width 15 is not where the missing throughput is. It reframes the drafter results: the community drafters were compared at a width that is not worth running, and Apathy's trained tail beating the lookup tail 14 times as a share is real but both tails are small contributions to a mode that gains about one percent at best. Caveats kept: one boot per arm, the eight-prompt cohort, matched contexts only.

ds7q void at model load, second cause (2026-09-05, 14:54Z by the shell clock). With the draft-quantization patch the loader got past its ValueError and died on AttributeError: 'Qwen3Config' object has no attribute 'hc_mult' while constructing DSparkDeepseekV4Model. vLLM 0.28's model registry (model_executor/models/registry.py:637-638) maps the architecture name "DSparkDraftModel", which the RadixArk checkpoint declares, to the DeepSeek V4 DSpark class, and registers the Qwen3 DSpark class (qwen3_dspark.py, Qwen3DSparkForCausalLM) as "Qwen3DSparkModel". So a DSpark drafter published for Qwen3.8 under the generic architecture name is built against the wrong model in vLLM 0.28 and cannot be served from its own config; two defects on the dspark path, both upstream, both hit before any kernel. For the test, a copy of the checkpoint with architectures set to Qwen3DSparkModel (safetensors hard-linked, config rewritten) is queued as ds7r (kernel on) and ds7rsa0 (kernel off) with the quantization patch, behind the remaining chains. ds7qsa0, armed before this was found, will die the same way and is left to its boundary.

Addendum (14:56Z by the shell clock; reported). The second DSpark defect reproduces on the 3090 identically, at the same registry line numbers (the first line number that has transferred between the two trees all session, a fact he notes only because he stopped assuming it), and his workaround matches this box's (a sibling directory, safetensors hard-linked with the inode verified, architectures the only key that differs). Two process catches of his, the same shape as his three earlier ones: an assert on the presence of an old path that was a prefix of the new one, so it passed either way; and a wait loop watching a process that had already finished, which would have started a second server on the card mid-cell. Five instances in one day of a check that reports success regardless of the state it checks; caught by reading output rather than exit status. Note item 15 carries both DSpark defects as upstream vLLM's with his sentence: a published Qwen3 DSpark drafter cannot be served by vLLM 0.28 as published, two independent patches before it loads, and neither error names the real cause. His ap15c (one sequence slot at K=15) still runs; his ds7 is queued behind it on the renamed copy.

Native ap15c and the deployability result (2026-09-05, 14:58Z by the shell clock; reported). ap15c (K=15, context 4096, MAX_SEQS=1) failed with 1.23 GiB available against 1.87 needed: one slot made the available memory smaller (1.41 to 1.23) while the requirement stayed 1.87, so the requirement is one request's need charged at admission and not a pool provisioned per slot, and the lever list is exhausted (the pin, the context, the slots, GPU_UTIL at 0.97; only about 0.99 remains, which nobody recommends). With the context ladder (2.23 at 8192, 1.87 at 4096) the fixed per-request mamba and gated-delta-net state is about 1.5 GiB. The result: on the same card at the same context and GPU_UTIL 0.97 at K=15, DFlash2 with its 1.2 GiB W4A16 head has 4.12 GiB of KV available and serves (7 FULL and 12 PIECEWISE captures), and the block-16 drafter with its 4.0 GiB bf16 head has 1.41 GiB and cannot serve at any context tried. A drafter published for exactly this target cannot be run at the width it was trained for on the 24 GiB card class most users have; card 0 reached it only through a 5.85 GB pin at a 57344 context that the 3090 cannot reproduce. The division of the arc's answer between the two cards is therefore the answer: card 0 has the acceptance result at K=15 (11 rows; the trained tail beats the lookup tail about 14 times as a share; the total still loses), the 3090 has the deployability result (it does not fit), and neither card could produce both. His ds7 was killed before it ran (exit 143, no rows; box verified clean, the per-cell sed did not persist) and is re-queued at 8192 on the renamed copy; at 3.5 GiB DSpark sits between the two heads and may fit where the block-16 drafter did not, which is itself a result.

## Native ds7: the symmetric comparison (2026-09-05, 15:09Z by the shell clock; reported)

On the 3090 at GPU_UTIL 0.97, an 8192 context, LOOKUP=0, the fork's verify kernel on, 32 rows each, engine-verified method dspark on the renamed-architecture copy with the quantization patch: DFlash2 (trained width 7) 3.832 tokens per step, 25.6 ms per step, 149.5 tok/s; DSpark (trained width 7) 3.374, 29.5, 114.3 tok/s, 76 percent of DFlash2's throughput; the block-16 drafter at half its width 3.000, 30.9, 96.9 tok/s, 65 percent. DSpark against DFlash2 paired by prompt and seed: -0.458 tokens per step (se 0.154, t -2.98, worse on 26 of 32). So our drafter wins its only like-for-like comparison, by 24 percent of throughput, with no width excuse available to either side; the target mismatch (W4A16, trained against neither) is shared. The per-position shape is the result neither seat expected: accepted tokens per position, DSpark 6749, 4688, 3365, 2444, 1767, 1328, 998 against DFlash2 6425, 4628, 3462, 2619, 2072, 1610, 1254, a ratio of 1.05, 1.01, 0.97, 0.93, 0.85, 0.82, 0.80 from position 0 to 6: ahead at the first position, level at the second, falling away from the third. The block-16 drafter's curve on this box does the same (212 against 201 at position 0, 24 against 43 by position 6), and so did MTP in arc two (1.00 to 0.60 across positions 0 to 6). Two independently trained community drafters of different architectures and authors, plus MTP, share one signature, stronger at the first token and weaker with depth, and the DFlash2 head is the only drafter of the four that does not; and DSpark, trained for exactly 7, decays the same way the block-16 drafter did, which retires the last width excuse: the depth behaviour is not a width artefact in either model. His one-sentence hypothesis, kept as one: the block-trained head holds its acceptance where the conditioning thins and the others do not. Completion lengths: the block-16 drafter produced two one-token rows there (prompt 5, seeds 2 and 4, the boundary seed again); DSpark none, DFlash2 none.

## The spill, measured (2026-09-05, 15:10Z by the shell clock)

spillA (the block-16 drafter on card 1 over-committed: the launcher's 5.2 GiB pin at 65536 beside the 4 GiB drafter, kernel off) reached startup at 15:10Z with "Initial free memory 22.45 GiB, reserved 5.2 GiB memory for KV" and 9 FULL and 12 PIECEWISE captures. The Windows GPU adapter memory counters (spill-counters.txt, sampled every ten seconds; card 1 is the adapter luid ending 4ca2, card 0 the one ending 3c6c): before the boot card 1 read dedicated 15274 MB and shared 88 MB while its weights loaded; as the pin was reserved, dedicated rose to 23364 MB (the card's ceiling below its 24564 MB total) and shared usage rose to 1818 MB, total committed 25182 MB, 618 MB more than the card holds, and stayed there through capture and startup (15:09:37 to 15:10:10). So an over-committed allocation on this WSL2 host runs with about 1.8 GB of the adapter's working set in host shared memory, which is the spill the record inferred from two symptom sets; it is now a counter reading with its baseline. The same counters caught card 0 at 15:09:13, between ap7p3's end and bl7p3's start, at dedicated 24136 MB, shared 3942 MB, committed 28078 MB: a teardown-and-load overlap over-committing that card by 3.5 GB for one sample, which is the kind of state lesson 99 warns "it booted" cannot exclude. What remains to pair with it: spillA's step cost (registered near 150) and spillB's flat shared usage with a step cost near 49.

Registered (2026-09-05, 15:13Z by the shell clock), on threadchip's point after the spill was measured: the counters caught card 0 over-committed by 3.5 GB for one sample between a cell's teardown and the next cell's load, the ordinary chain pattern all day, so some of this box's earlier numbers may have been taken with part of a working set in host memory and nothing in their logs would show it. The adapter counters now sample continuously (spill-counters-continuous.txt, every ten seconds), every later cell boots behind an idle gate (card 0 under 500 MiB used, then thirty seconds), and the three load-bearing 4090 numbers of arc two are re-measured under the counters in a seventh chain: w15chk (std1's configuration, the query-maximum-16 cost, 56.5 expected), d7chk (the launcher default, 23.2 expected) and mix7chk (the forced slow-class boot, 45 expected). Readings: shared usage at its baseline and dedicated below the ceiling through the rows means the arc-two numbers stand and the 3090's flat results remain their control; shared usage elevated through the rows means that number was taken spilled and arc two is corrected at its source; a spill under mix7chk and none under d7chk would give the slow-boot state a memory face. The block-16 drafter boots at 146 to 164 are already read as over-committed, consistent.

Addendum (15:21Z by the shell clock; threadchip's last two points, adopted). The line the spill draws: it moves the working set, not the computation, so it affects speed and not numerics; every acceptance column from a spilled cell stands as read, including ap15sa0's trained tail at 8.3 percent of accepted tokens, the arc's answer to the question asked, and only the step costs from those cells are void; the discovery costs the arc almost nothing because it invalidates numbers already unquoted and leaves the ones the answer rests on untouched. And the record now has three tiers of 4090 numbers, which the write-up states rather than lets the counters' existence imply away: measured clean under the counters (bl7p3, ap7p3 by the first sampler, and the seventh chain when it lands), measured spilled (spillA, ap11sa0b, and by their configuration ap7sa0, ap11sa0, ap15sa0: step costs void, acceptance intact), and measured before the counters existed, the largest tier, all of arc two included, not wrong but not checked, with the check now cheap and the three load-bearing numbers being re-measured first. The 3090 has no third tier: the card cannot spill, so every native number in both arcs was taken in a state the counters would call clean, which is why its results serve as controls, not because the card is faster or slower but because its failure mode is loud.

## ap11sa0b: the engine's error, captured (2026-09-05, 15:33Z by the shell clock)

The width-11 re-run ended at 13 rows like its predecessor, and this time the client recorded the cause: at request 14 (prompt 5 seed 2, the boundary seed) the engine core died with "torch.AcceleratorError: CUDA error: an illegal memory access was encountered", every later request returned 500 (ROW_ERROR lines for prompts 5, 6 and 7 of seed 2), and the server then went away (connection refused on the metrics call, which the client did not yet catch). The boot was spilled (card 0's adapter at shared 1934 MB through its rows) and the fork's verify kernel was off. So three cells in a row on the block-16 drafter have died in the second seed's pass (ap11sa0 and ap11sa0b at prompt 5 seed 2, ap15sa0 at prompt 3 seed 2), all over-committed and spilled, all with the kernel off, and the arc's first illegal memory access (ap7 at initialisation) was also an over-committed boot; meanwhile both clean K=7 boots of this drafter (ap7p3 with the kernel on, spillB with it off) ran prompt 5 seed 2 to 1024. The reading with support: the illegal memory access is the spilled state's face on this path, not the fork's kernel (off in all three decode-time cases), and it surfaces on the requests where the drafter's numerics sit at a boundary. The falsifier, registered before it runs: ap11p3, the same drafter at K=11 with the kernel on at the fitting pin (3 GiB, 8192; the pin that ran K=7 clean); surviving prompt 5 seed 2 says the crashes were the spill, dying there with the counters clean says the plain-DFlash path has a fault at K above 7 on that prompt. The client now also catches a refused connection and ends the cell with a line instead of a traceback.

Provenance note (15:34Z by the shell clock). The flightbench checkout that holds the raw cells is on its branch clibench (every v028 commit of this window is there), and until this minute the arc-two and arc-three cell outputs, late captures, chain logs and the arc-two arm scripts were on disk only, never added to git; they are now committed on that branch (flightbench clibench 668f86b), so the record's claim that every local number can be recomputed from the ROW lines holds from the repository and not only from this disk. Files belonging to other work in the same directory (mission logs, server logs, trace files, other arm scripts) were left as they were.

Addendum (15:36Z by the shell clock; threadchip's correction and hypothesis, with one fact of this box's against the confound). His correction: the short rows are not specific to the block-16 drafter; on the 3090, DFlash2 under the forced Triton configuration returned one token on prompt 5 seeds 2 and 3, and the block-16 drafter on prompt 5 seeds 2 and 4, so prompt 5 seed 2 is distinguished across two arcs, two drafters and three perturbations (a forced kernel configuration, a different drafter, a memory spill), and arc two's "a boundary seed that any numerics change moves" looks like the general case. His hypothesis, kept as one: that request sits at a decision boundary where the continuation is nearly degenerate, any numerics change tips it, and the outcome of tipping depends on the memory state, a one-token completion when clean and a fault when spilled. His confound: this box's three decode-time faults were all in the second seed's pass, so the request identity and the depth into the pass are entangled. This box's rows loosen it a little: two faults were at prompt 5 seed 2 (request 14) and one at prompt 3 seed 2 (request 12), so the fault follows neither the boundary request exactly nor a fixed request number; what the three share is the second pass and about ten minutes of decoding into a spilled boot. ap11p3 (the fitting pin) tests the spill dependence first; a cell with the seeds in a different order would separate the request from the depth, registered as a follow-up and not run tonight. On the clean 3090 the request runs to completion (one token, no fault) on both drafters, so the spill is producing the fault, not revealing one.

DSpark at the shipped defaults, both cards (2026-09-05, 15:54Z by the shell clock; the 3090 numbers from threadchip's engine lines, relayed). ds7def on the 3090 (method dspark, 65536, utilization 0.93, the 5583457484-byte pin, max_num_seqs 8): ValueError at the KV allocation, 7.05 GiB needed for one 65536-token request against 5.2 available. The shipped head at the same pin and context, from the bl7 boots that served: a 68,605-token pool, concurrency 1.05, so 4.97 GiB per request. At the shipped defaults (the 5.20 GiB pin, a 65536 context, width 7) on the 3090, from the engine's own lines: beside the 1.2 GiB head one 65536-token request needs 4.97 GiB of KV cache and serves with about five percent of the pin to spare; beside the 3.5 GiB DSpark head the same request needs 7.05 GiB and the boot fails at that allocation; the requirement moved by 2.08 GiB when only the drafter changed. So a bigger drafter is charged twice, once as resident weights and again as a larger per-request KV requirement, and the shipped pin is sized for the small one with a five percent margin, which is also why a width-15 boot of the shipped head failed by ten mebibytes earlier in the day. That the requirement grew by about the size of the weights difference is two points, not a law; the mechanism is the measurement. Both community drafters fail at the defaults on both cards. Erratum on this box: ds7r's and ds7rsa0's ValueError lines were not read whole before their containers were removed (the capture caught the first seventy characters of the line), so card 0's defaults row records the death and points at the 3090's numbers for the requirement, which is set by the model and the drafter, not the card; from this entry on every cell's engine log is copied out of its container each minute (serverlog-<cell>.txt in the raw directory), so a line survives the container that produced it.

w15chk and d7chk landed, the first two re-checks under the counters (2026-09-05, 16:07Z by the shell clock; chk-w15chk.txt, chk-d7chk.txt, counters_window.py over spill-counters-continuous.txt, engine logs in serverlog-w15chk.txt and serverlog-d7chk.txt). w15chk (std1 configuration: width 15, kernel on, lookup on, default pin, 57344 resolved), 32 rows, steady 56.5 ms per step (56.1 to 57.4), 3.727 tokens per step, 65.7 tok/s: arc two's 56.5 reproduced to the decimal. Its counters through the rows, every minute from 15:50 to 15:57: dedicated 23363 to 23371 MB, shared 512, committed 23876 to 23884, flat, so about 430 MB of its working set sat off-card from the first row, a steady over-commit rather than an accumulating one (threadchip's point: a steady off-card residency is the shape a constant 2.4x would have). d7chk (the default: width 7, 65536, the same pin), 32 rows, steady 23.2 ms per step (23.0 to 23.8), 3.708 tokens per step, 158.0 tok/s: arc two's 23.2 reproduced; its counters through the rows, 16:01 to 16:03: dedicated 23306 to 23314, shared 96, committed 23403 to 23411, the baseline. So the default stands as a clean number, and the width-15 number was measured with about half a gigabyte of its working set in host memory; whether that half-gigabyte is what costs 2.4x is untested until w15p3 (registered above; readings: near 56 = the kernel cost is real and the residency incidental; 23 to 30 = item 9's 4090 number was the residency; 35 to 50 = both, and one pair cannot apportion them, a second boot at each profile before anyone does, threadchip supplying the native half). Two things the pair shows on its own: the extra working set of the width-15 configuration is what crosses the line (graph capture took 1.67 GiB against 1.36 at width 7, and the committed totals differ by 473 MB), and the dedicated ceiling on this host is about 23.37 GB of the card's 24.56, so the default configuration sits within about 470 MB of that ceiling and any larger working set, a wider block, a bigger drafter, a raised pin, goes into host memory rather than failing. That is the mechanism under every WSL2 memory finding of both arcs in one sentence. Both boots captured normally (3 s); mix7chk (the forced slow class, head artefact held) is on its rows behind the same gate.

The plain-DFlash residual was never a matched cross-card comparison (2026-09-05, 16:14Z by the shell clock; threadchip's classification of its own boots from its logs, relayed, and the captured sequences here). ap7p3's captured sequence reads L D:dflash_head C: backbone artefact loaded, drafter head compiled in-process, the mixed boot arc two calls the slow class; the first sampler had its rows at dedicated 23361 / shared 214 / committed 23575, about 130 MiB over this host's dedicated ceiling. The 3090's ap7g and bl7g were both full compiles (backbone and head compiled, no loads), matched to each other, so its +5.3 ms stands within the card; the 4090's +26.1 compared a mixed boot against bl7p3, so the "2.1x here against 1.2x there, mechanism unknown" sentence compared boot classes, not hosts, and is withdrawn from item 14 and the write-up as of this entry (the within-card numbers stay). The same scan found the 3090's bl15g was a mixed boot (one load, two compiles) at 26.2 ms per step, so the arc-two result that the mixed class costs the native card nothing holds at width 15 under arc-three conditions, found by accident rather than by design. ap7p3b (ap7p3 repeated unchanged, the head artefact now in the cache) is queued as the eleventh chain, readings registered in its script: L L with shared at baseline and 25 to 35 ms per step = the residual was the boot class and item 14 folds into the arc-two state; about 49 with both confounders removed = a real path cost, with the 3090's 30.9 as its native contrast. mix7chk, the forced slow-class boot of the shipped head, is on its rows at 45.0 ms per step with dedicated at the ceiling and shared 358, so the slow class has a memory face of about 260 MiB over the ceiling; its entry follows when the rows are in.

mix7chk landed, the third re-check (2026-09-05, 16:15Z by the shell clock; chk-mix7chk.txt, serverlog-mix7chk.txt, counters_window.py 16:05 to 16:14). The forced slow-class boot of the shipped head (the default lineage's head artefact held in the cache volume, so the backbone loaded and the head compiled in-process; captured sequence L D:dflash_head C L; capture 4 s, 1.12 GiB), 32 rows: steady 45.0 ms per step (44.7 to 45.5), 3.798 tokens per step, 83.8 tok/s, arc two's 45 reproduced. Counters through the rows, every minute from 16:08 to 16:14: dedicated 23363 to 23371 (the ceiling), shared 358, committed 23721 to 23729, flat. Against d7chk (the same configuration, all three graphs loaded): the mixed boot carries 318 MiB more committed memory and sits about 260 MiB over the dedicated ceiling where the loaded boot sits about 50 MiB under it. So the three re-checks read: the default clean at 23.2; the width-15 boot at 56.5 with 420 MiB over; the slow-class boot at 45.0 with 260 MiB over. Both of arc two's 4090-only slow states coincide with the working set crossing the dedicated ceiling, and neither clean number does. Coincidence is not cause: w15p3 (width 15 with room) and ap7p3b (a plain-DFlash boot that should load its head) are the two cells that give each state room and see whether the cost stays; the slow class with room is not yet queued, because forcing it at the fitting profile needs that lineage's head artefact identified and held, and it is registered as the next cell after those two if the first two say memory. The seventh chain is done; the eighth (ap11p3) boots behind the gate now.

ap11p3 landed, the fault falsifier (2026-09-05, 16:41Z by the shell clock; chk-ap11p3.txt, serverlog-ap11p3.txt, counters 16:15 to 16:28; step costs from analyze_drafters.py, which from this entry excludes boundary rows, out under 16, from the step-cost mean, since a two-token completion is one step whose wall is the first-step latency; the earlier 49.4 for ap7p3 stands under that rule, and chk_summary.py follows it). The block-16 drafter at width 11 with the fork verify kernel on at the fitting pin (3 GiB, 8192; loaded 18.09 GiB, reserved 2.79, capture 6 s and 1.32 GiB, full compile), 32 rows, exit 0: no fault anywhere in the cohort. Prompt 5 seed 2, the request on which two of the three spilled kernel-off boots died, ran to 1024 tokens at 4.580 tokens per step; prompt 5 seed 4 returned 2 tokens, the third card-0 cell of this drafter to do so. Counters through the rows: dedicated 23361 to 23391, shared 278, committed 23639 to 23669, so this boot sat about 190 MiB over the dedicated ceiling, not with room. Steady 54.4 ms per step (53.7 to 56.0), 3.319 tokens per step, 60.3 tok/s. Acceptance paired against bl7p3 (same memory profile, the shipped head at width 7): -0.454 tokens per step (se 0.181, t -2.50, worse on 26 of 32), against bl7off -0.409 and against the default -0.479; per-position per thousand rounds 211 134 86 58 41 31 24 19 14 11 8, the trained tail (positions 7 to 10) 52 of 637 accepted, 8.2 percent, the same share the width-15 cell gave. Reading: at the fitting pin with the kernel on, the width-11 configuration runs the whole cohort where three spilled kernel-off boots faulted, which is what the registration called the spill producing the fault; the kernel setting differs too (the faulting boots had it off), so the separation is not complete, and ap11p3sa0 (the same boot with the kernel off) is queued as a twelfth chain to close it.

ds7rp3 landed, DSpark on card 0 at the fitting profile, the first community-drafter boot on this card with its working set under the ceiling (2026-09-05, 16:41Z by the shell clock; drf-ds7rp3.txt, serverlog-ds7rp3.txt, counters 16:29 to 16:38). The renamed copy with the quant patch and the method sed, kernel on, 3 GiB pin, 8192, LOOKUP=0: loaded 17.58 GiB, reserved 2.79, capture 5 s and 1.36 GiB, captured sequence D:backbone C D:dspark_head C (a full compile, the class threadchip's native ds7 was). Counters through the rows, 16:33 to 16:37: dedicated 22930, shared 86, committed 23017, about 440 MiB under the ceiling and at the shared baseline. 32 rows: steady 27.5 ms per step (27.2 to 27.9), 3.191 tokens per step, 115.1 tok/s. Paired against bl7p3 (the shipped head at the same profile, 3.773 and 23.3): -0.582 tokens per step (se 0.119, t -4.91, worse on 28 of 32); against bl7off -0.536, against the default -0.606; per-position per thousand rounds 197 134 94 68 52 39 29 against the head's level curve, the same depth signature the 3090 saw. The symmetric comparison is now two-card and both halves are clean and class-matched (full compiles on both): DSpark loses acceptance by 0.58 here and 0.46 natively, costs 27.5 against 23.3 ms per step here (1.18 times) and 29.5 against 25.6 there (1.15 times), and serves 115.1 tok/s here against 114 there, with the shipped head at 160.4 here against 150. When the boot class and the memory state are matched the two hosts agree on the drafter to a few percent, which is the strongest statement this arc has that the earlier cross-host disagreements were class and memory, not hosts.

Two corrections on the same hour (2026-09-05, 16:43Z by the shell clock; threadchip's, adopted). First, the fault sentence: ap11p3 ran clean while its counters read about 190 MiB over the dedicated ceiling, so being over the line is not by itself sufficient for the decode-time fault; the three faulting boots were both far over it (1.8 to 1.9 GB of working set in host memory) and kernel-off, and ap11p3 differs from them in both magnitude and kernel setting. The registration called ap11p3 the fitting-pin check; the counters say it was a boot 190 MiB over the line, so it tests the kernel-on path at a small overage, not the on-card state. ap11p3sa0 (kernel off at the same overage) says whether the kernel setting is the discriminator; a boot of this drafter with real room (a 2 GiB pin) would test the magnitude, registered as a follow-up. Second, the boundary-row rule applied to the native numbers: threadchip's ap7g moves from 30.9 to 30.6 ms per step (two one-token rows out), bl7g and ds7 unchanged; nothing changes by more than 0.3 ms and the record keeps the figures as first reported with this note. The convergence stated from both sides (threadchip's table, relayed; tok/s from each seat's own rows): DSpark 114.4 tok/s on the 3090 and 116.0 on card 0 by threadchip's computation (115.1 as the row mean here), step ratios to the shipped head 1.15 and 1.18, paired acceptance -0.458 and -0.582, fractions of the head's throughput 76 and 72 percent, the last differing because the head itself runs 161.9 here against 149.7 there. Every cross-host disagreement chased today (the 5x, the 2.1x residual, the fault, the capture difference) came from boot class or memory state being unmatched; with both matched the hosts agree on a drafter to a few percent, and that sentence now closes item 14.

w15p3 landed: the width-15 cost on this host was the memory overage, and posted item 9's 4090 headline is wrong (2026-09-05, 16:49Z by the shell clock; drf-w15p3.txt, serverlog-w15p3.txt, counters 16:38 to 16:46, pairing script inline against chk-w15chk, drf-bl7p3 and chk-d7chk). Width 15, the fork verify kernel on, lookup on (the fork default, as std1 and w15chk ran), 3 GiB pin, 8192; loaded 15.35 GiB, reserved 2.79, capture 6 s and 1.91 GiB, captured sequence D:backbone C D:dflash_head C D:dflash2_candidate_selector C (a full compile). Counters through the rows, 16:43 to 16:45: dedicated 21366 to 21374, shared 86, committed 21453 to 21461, about 2 GB under the dedicated ceiling and at the shared baseline. 32 rows: steady 23.4 ms per step (23.1 to 23.9, median 23.3), 3.737 tokens per step, 157.8 tok/s. Paired against w15chk (the same width and kernel at the default pin, 420 MiB over the ceiling, 56.5 ms per step): acceptance +0.010 tokens per step (se 0.088, t +0.11), the same numerics, and 157.8 against 65.7 tok/s, 2.4 times, from the memory state alone. Paired against bl7p3 (width 7 at the same profile): -0.036 tokens per step (se 0.084, t -0.43), 157.8 against 160.4 tok/s; against d7chk (the default): +0.030 and 157.8 against 158.0. So with its working set on the card, width 15 costs this host nothing per step and buys nothing in throughput, which is the 3090's result (26.2 against 25.6; 151.6 against 149.5). The registered reading is the second one: item 9's 4090 number (56.5 against 23, 2.4x, five boots) was the width-15 working set crossing the dedicated ceiling by about 420 MiB, not the split-KV verify attention; the kernel is exonerated, and the QMAX dependence in item 9 (a matched width-7 cell at QMAX 16 read 63.5, 9 and 12 read 23.2 and 23.4, so "somewhere between 13 and 16") reads now as the buffer growth crossing the same line, which q16p3 (that cell at the fitting profile, thirteenth chain, readings registered in its script) tests directly before the correction is worded as certain on that clause. What survives of item 9: the 3090's flat pair, the SPEC_ATTN=0 boundary-seed observation, the 10 MiB KV edge, and the stacking sentence, though the stacking's 56.5 and 63.5 are overage numbers. The correction goes to the fork under Michael's pen; a draft is in the note file under the posted items. Four controls before overturning a posted record: the counters fire on the overage (w15chk 512, spillA 1818), stay quiet without it (d7chk 96, bl7p3 86, w15p3 86), the raw captures are the counter files, and the discriminator is w15p3 itself, the same width and kernel with only the memory profile changed.
